  5. **Expected events:** GF-10.A — one `PROPOSED_QTY`. GF-10.B — `ENTER_LONG` at bar 0, then `LIQUIDATION_PARTIAL` at **bar 1**, **bar 2 and bar 3**, and **no** liquidation event at bar 4. GF-10.C — `ENTER_LONG` at bar 0 and `MARGIN_MODEL_UNAVAILABLE` recorded once at bar 0; no liquidation on any of the five bars.
  6. **Expected fills/prices/quantities:**
     - GF-10.A — `max_qty = (1000 × 2) / (100 × 1) = 20`, and because the raw risk-at-stop quantity `1000 × 1.0 / (|100 − 50| × 1) = 20` equals the cap, the outcome is `proposed_qty = 20`; raising `requested_risk_fraction` to `1.5` makes the raw quantity `30`, which **exceeds** the cap and must yield `REJECTED(reason=LEVERAGE_CAP)`, not `20`.
     - **GF-10.B — the decided `PARTIAL_TO_MAINTENANCE` rule, stated as arithmetic. The whole ledger was recomputed in repair round 6; the round-5 chain was wrong from bar 2 onward and the error is named below rather than silently replaced.** The entry fill is `20 @ 100.00` at bar 0, fixed by `entry_qty_override: 20`; the override exists because at `bucket_capital 250` the sizer would propose `5` (`250 × 1.0 / |100 − 50| = 5`, and the leverage cap `(250 × 2) / 100 = 5` agrees), and this sub-fixture needs a position the bucket cannot carry.
     - **The equity identity the whole ledger runs on, stated once.** At a mark `m`, after a sequence of liquidations, equity is

       ```
       equity(m) = bucket_capital
                 + Σ q_k × (m_k − entry_price)          [realized on each liquidated slice, at the mark it was liquidated at]
                 + qty_remaining × (m − entry_price)     [unrealized on what is still open]
                 − Σ fee_k                               [cumulative liquidation fees]
       ```

       and the maintenance requirement at that mark is `maintenance_margin_rate × qty_remaining × m`. **This is the term round 5 dropped.** Its bar-2 line read `250 − 1.1616 + 17.58 × (92 − 100) = 108.1984`, which prices the remaining `17.58` from the entry but never books the realized loss on the `2.42` units liquidated at `96.00` — `2.42 × (96 − 100) = −9.68`. Every figure downstream of that omission was wrong, including the assertion that bar 3 does not liquidate. The corrected chain is below, and it is cross-checked two ways at every step: forward from the identity above, and incrementally from the previous bar's post-event equity by `qty_remaining × Δmark`.
     - **Bar 1, mark `96.00`.** Equity before the event is `250 + 20 × (96 − 100) = 170.00`; the maintenance requirement is `0.10 × 20 × 96 = 192.00`; `170.00 < 192.00`, so the rule fires. The rule liquidates the **least** `qty_step`-rounded quantity `x` that restores coverage after its own fee:

       ```
       250 + 20 × (96 − 100) − x × 96 × 0.005  >=  (20 − x) × 96 × 0.10
       ```

       Rearranged, `170.00 − 0.48·x >= 192.00 − 9.6·x`, i.e. `9.12·x >= 22.00`, so the raw threshold is `x = 22 / 9.12 = 2.412280701754…`. Rounded **adverse** — away from the trader, i.e. ceiled — to the `0.01` quantity step, the asserted liquidated quantity is **`2.42`**. Fee `2.42 × 96 × 0.005 = 1.1616`; post-event equity `170.00 − 1.1616 = 168.8384`; remaining quantity `20 − 2.42 = 17.58`; new maintenance requirement `0.10 × 17.58 × 96 = 168.768`; coverage restored because `168.8384 >= 168.768`. **Minimality is asserted too:** at `x = 2.41` the fee is `1.1568`, equity `168.8432`, remaining quantity `17.59` and maintenance `168.864`, so `168.8432 < 168.864` and `2.41` is rejected.
     - **Bar 2, mark `92.00`.** Equity before the event, from the identity: `250 + 2.42 × (96 − 100) + 17.58 × (92 − 100) − 1.1616 = 250 − 9.68 − 140.64 − 1.1616 = `**`98.5184`**. Incremental cross-check: `168.8384 + 17.58 × (92 − 96) = 168.8384 − 70.32 = 98.5184` ✓. Maintenance is `0.10 × 17.58 × 92 = 161.736`; `98.5184 < 161.736`, so the rule fires. `98.5184 − 0.46·x >= 161.736 − 9.2·x` gives `8.74·x >= 63.2176`, raw `x = 63.2176 / 8.74 = 7.233135…`, adverse-ceiled to **`7.24`**. Fee `7.24 × 92 × 0.005 = 3.3304`; post-event equity `98.5184 − 3.3304 = 95.1880`; remaining quantity `17.58 − 7.24 = 10.34`; maintenance `0.10 × 10.34 × 92 = 95.128`; `95.1880 >= 95.128` ✓. Minimality: at `x = 7.23` the fee is `3.3258`, equity `95.1926`, remaining quantity `10.35` and maintenance `95.22`, so `95.1926 < 95.22` and `7.23` is rejected. **Round 5 asserted `6.13` here, from the `108.1984` that omitted bar 1's realized `−9.68`; `6.13` is withdrawn.**
     - **Bar 3, mark `88.00`.** Equity before the event: `250 + 2.42 × (96 − 100) + 7.24 × (92 − 100) + 10.34 × (88 − 100) − (1.1616 + 3.3304) = 250 − 9.68 − 57.92 − 124.08 − 4.4920 = `**`53.828`**. Incremental cross-check: `95.1880 + 10.34 × (88 − 92) = 95.1880 − 41.36 = 53.828` ✓. Maintenance is `0.10 × 10.34 × 88 = `**`90.992`**; `53.828 < 90.992`, so the rule fires **again** — which is the assertion round 5 got backwards. `53.828 − 0.44·x >= 90.992 − 8.8·x` gives `8.36·x >= 37.164`, raw `x = 37.164 / 8.36 = 4.445454…`, adverse-ceiled to **`4.45`**. Fee `4.45 × 88 × 0.005 = 1.958`; post-event equity `53.828 − 1.958 = 51.870`; remaining quantity `10.34 − 4.45 = 5.89`; maintenance `0.10 × 5.89 × 88 = 51.832`; `51.870 >= 51.832` ✓. Minimality: at `x = 4.44` the fee is `1.9536`, equity `51.8744`, remaining quantity `5.90` and maintenance `51.92`, so `51.8744 < 51.92` and `4.44` is rejected. **Round 5's "no liquidation, ending quantity `11.45`" is withdrawn in both halves.**
     - **Bar 4, mark `88.00` — the non-firing checkpoint.** No trade and no mark change, so equity is unchanged at `51.870` and the requirement is unchanged at `0.10 × 5.89 × 88 = 51.832`; `51.870 >= 51.832`, so **no liquidation occurs**. The margin of `0.038` is exactly the residue the bar-3 adverse ceil left (`(4.45 − 4.445454…) × 8.36 = 0.038`), and asserting it is what proves the rule both restores coverage and stops.
     - GF-10.C — no fill other than the entry, on any of the five bars.
  7. **Expected end state / reasons:** typed reasons `LEVERAGE_CAP`, `LIQUIDATION_PARTIAL`, `MARGIN_MODEL_UNAVAILABLE`. GF-10.B asserts the full ledger, one row per bar, every cell derived in field 6:

     | Bar | Mark | Equity before | Maintenance before | Fires? | `x` (raw → adverse-ceil) | Fee | Equity after | Qty after | Maintenance after |
     |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
     | 0 | `100.00` | `250.0000` | `200.000` | **no** — entry only | — | — | `250.0000` | `20.00` | `200.000` |
     | 1 | `96.00` | `170.0000` | `192.000` | **yes** | `2.412280701754…` → `2.42` | `1.1616` | `168.8384` | `17.58` | `168.768` |
     | 2 | `92.00` | `98.5184` | `161.736` | **yes** | `7.233135…` → `7.24` | `3.3304` | `95.1880` | `10.34` | `95.128` |
     | 3 | `88.00` | `53.8280` | `90.992` | **yes** | `4.445454…` → `4.45` | `1.9580` | `51.8700` | `5.89` | `51.832` |
     | 4 | `88.00` | `51.8700` | `51.832` | **no** | — | — | `51.8700` | `5.89` | `51.832` |

     Every "maintenance" cell is `maintenance_margin_rate × qty × mark` at **that row's own mark**, so the bar-0 row is `0.10 × 20 × 100 = 200.000` against equity `250.0000` — the position is solvent at entry, and the first breach is genuinely bar 1's. The run ends with the position **open at quantity `5.89`** and the following closing decomposition, each term asserted separately so a single wrong term cannot hide inside the total: realized `−121.00` from the three liquidated slices (`2.42 × (96 − 100) = −9.68`, `7.24 × (92 − 100) = −57.92`, `4.45 × (88 − 100) = −53.40`); unrealized `−70.68` on the still-open `5.89` at the mark `88.00`; **cumulative liquidation fees `6.4500`** (`1.1616 + 3.3304 + 1.9580`); and therefore final equity `250.00 − 121.00 − 70.68 − 6.4500 = 51.8700`, which reconciles with the ledger row for bar 4. No `MARGIN_MODEL_UNAVAILABLE` appears anywhere in its artifact. In GF-10.C the position remains open at bar 4 with its original quantity `20` and the artifact carries exactly one `MARGIN_MODEL_UNAVAILABLE` record.
  8. **Discriminator (RED):** run GF-10.C against A with `tw_audit_semantics_mode: "research"`, `tw_margin_call_mode: "tradingview"` — A fabricates a margin call from leverage-derived percentages with no venue schedule present (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/runner.py:563-564`, `:1445-1450`; `MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/config.py:619-624`). Second RED: run GF-10.C against B, whose hard-coded `1.104` / `0.97` maintenance multipliers liquidate with no schedule at all (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/engine/mtc_runner.py:644-649`, `:694-705`). Both must record a liquidation where the decided rule records `MARGIN_MODEL_UNAVAILABLE`.

### C11 — Fixed, ATR, and swing stops

- **A behaviour:** percent, ATR and swing+ATR stops, each adverse-rounded; a missing ATR or a missing swing reference returns `None`, i.e. **no stop** (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/exits.py:186-210`). The swing reference is drawn from `_swing_history`, and the current bar is appended to that history only at the **end** of the bar loop, so the decision bar is excluded (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/runner.py:1241-1249`, `:1043`). Entry readiness requires the ATR trackers to be warm and the swing window to be full (`:1262-1275`). At exit time a standing stop fills at the stop price on a touch and at the **open** when the bar gaps beyond it (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/exits.py:395-421`).
- **B behaviour:** a NaN ATR is replaced by the literal `0.01` (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/modules/risk/sl_calculator.py:94-95`), a NaN swing ATR buffer by `0` (`:152-153`), and the swing window is `[bar_index − swing_lookback, bar_index]` inclusive — it **includes the decision bar** (`:135-142`).
- **Pine reference:** stop calculation uses completed prior bars (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/01_PINE/MTC_V2.pine:283-309`).
- **Disagreement:** B leaks the decision bar into its own stop and fabricates a `0.01` ATR when the indicator is not warm; either change the stop distance, and through C07 the quantity.
- **Canonical semantics and reason:** percent and ATR stops use finite completed-bar inputs; the swing lookback **excludes the decision bar**. A missing ATR or an incomplete swing window **blocks the entry** with a typed reason — it never fabricates a distance. A protective stop is a standing order active from the next tradable instant after entry: a touch fills at the stop, and a gap beyond it fills at the adverse open.
- **Chosen implementation:** **A-corrected**, only to make next-instant activation and the failure reasons explicit.

All runs `tp_mode: "None"`, gates disabled.

### C12 — Percent, ATR, and R targets

- **A behaviour:** targets are built into a working-exit book with half-up tick rounding, and `tp_mode == "None"` produces no target at all (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/exits.py:213-233`). Standing targets fill at the target on a touch and at the **open** on a favourable gap (`:450-493`).
- **B behaviour:** an R-mode target with no stop **silently becomes a percent target** using the configured `percent`, whose default is `2.0` (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/modules/risk/tp_calculator.py:163-165`; `MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/config/defaults.py:94`), and a NaN ATR becomes the literal `0.01` (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/modules/risk/tp_calculator.py:132-133`).
- **Pine reference:** target calculation at `MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/01_PINE/MTC_V2.pine:311-319`.
- **Disagreement:** B changes the target **method** when an input is missing, so a run labelled "R target" can silently be a percent target.
- **Canonical semantics and reason:** the configured target method is never substituted. A missing ATR or a missing stop **blocks target creation** with a typed reason. Targets are standing orders: a favourable gap fills at the open, otherwise at the target price.
- **Chosen implementation:** **A**.

R2 — `BLOCK(reason=TARGET_UNAVAILABLE_MISSING_STOP)`, no target and no entry order. R3 — `BLOCK(reason=TARGET_UNAVAILABLE_ATR_NOT_READY)`.

### C13 — MultiTP

- **A behaviour:** the working-exit book holds ordered legs; the nearer target is evaluated first, a `TP1` fill sets `continue_evaluation_this_bar` so the remainder is still evaluated on the same bar, and a `TP2` fill closes `exit_pct = 1.0` of the remainder (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/exits.py:450-493`). The runner loops while that flag is set (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/runner.py:565-590`), and the basket owner keeps one lifecycle across partial closes (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/position_manager.py:254-355`).
- **B behaviour:** with Multi-TP on, a stop can realise **two closed trade records** — the TP1 leg and the remainder — and the stop may be deferred behind a TP fill (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/engine/mtc_runner.py:2255-2263`, `:2413-2430`).
- **Pine reference:** the TP1/TP2 close-only lifecycle is `MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/01_PINE/MTC_V2.pine:1204-1348`.
- **Disagreement:** trade-record granularity differs, so the same economics produce different trade counts and therefore different per-trade statistics.
- **Canonical semantics and reason:** one position lifecycle may contain several execution fragments. TP1 closes `initial_or_rebased_open_qty × tp1_fraction`, capped by the current quantity; TP2 closes the entire remainder. Fees and PnL accrue per fill, but metrics carry **one lifecycle ID**. The stop stays active for the remainder. Same-bar ambiguity follows C19.
- **Chosen implementation:** **A** lifecycle model.
- **GF-13**
  1. **Type:** bar-driven.
  2. **Config:** `tp_mode: "Multi"`, `tp1_r_multiple: 1.0`, `tp1_close_pct: 40.0`, `tp2_r_multiple: 2.0`, `use_sl: true`, `use_sl_percent: true`, `sl_percent: 5.0`, `execution_profile_id: "touch"` semantics per C20.
  3. **Frozen metadata:** `instrument_price_tick 0.01`, `instrument_qty_step 1.0`, `instrument_contract_multiplier 1`; entry quantity fixed at `10` by `sizing_method: FIXED_QTY, requested_fixed_qty: 10`.
  4. **Bars:** `0 | 2026-01-01T00:00:00Z | 100,100,100,100,1` raw `(1,0)`; run R1 `1 | …:01 | 101,106,101,104,1`, `2 | …:02 | 104,111,104,110,1`; run R2 replaces bar 1 with `112,112,112,112,1`.
  5. **Expected events:** R1 — `ENTER_LONG 10 @ 100` with `stop 95.00`, `TP1 105.00`, `TP2 110.00`; bar 1 `FILL_TP1`; bar 2 `FILL_TP2` and `LIFECYCLE_CLOSED`. R2 — bar 1 emits `FILL_TP1` then `FILL_TP2` in that order, both at the same open, then `LIFECYCLE_CLOSED`.
  6. **Expected fills/prices/quantities:** R1 — TP1 fills `4` at `105.00` leaving `6`; TP2 fills `6` at `110.00`. R2 — TP1 fills `4` at `112.00`, TP2 fills `6` at `112.00`; two fills, one lifecycle, total closed quantity exactly `10` with no phantom remainder.
  7. **Expected end state / reasons:** typed reasons `TP1`, `TP2`; the metrics layer reports **one** closed lifecycle in both runs.
  8. **Discriminator (RED):** R2 against B, which records two closed-trade rows and therefore two lifecycles for the same economics (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/engine/mtc_runner.py:2255-2263`). A second RED mutates the TP1 quantity to `initial_qty` instead of `initial_qty × tp1_fraction`, producing a total closed quantity of `16` against an opened `10`.

### C14 — Break-even

- **A behaviour:** `update_protective_stop_owner` runs **before** `evaluate_price_exit` on the same bar (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/runner.py:551-558`, `:565-567`), so a break-even revision is already live for that bar's own stop test. BE is skipped when trailing owns the stop (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/exits.py:332`), the trigger probe is the bar **high** for a long unless close-only execution or the `tradingview` legacy mode is selected (`:334-339`), and the revision is monotonic (`:340-348`).
- **B behaviour:** BE is evaluated **after** the stop/target checks, so its revision affects the next bar (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/engine/mtc_runner.py:2450-2463`, and the ordering note at `:2201-2207`), but the trigger probe is the intrabar extreme, `high` for a long (`:2454-2457`).
- **Pine reference:** close-triggered BE with monotonic stop updates (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/01_PINE/MTC_V2.pine:1143-1172`).
- **Disagreement:** two independent axes are in dispute — the trigger source (intrabar extreme versus completed close) and the effective bar (same bar versus next). A's combination, an intrabar trigger that is effective on the trigger bar, is the one that can turn a wick into an exit that never happened.
- **Canonical semantics and reason:** the BE trigger is evaluated at the **completed close** using entry-to-initial-stop R. A revision becomes active on the **next** bar, never retroactively on the trigger bar. It may only tighten. When trailing is enabled and active, trailing owns stop revision. Reason: a stop that moves on the strength of the same bar it is then tested against is indistinguishable from lookahead.
- **Chosen implementation:** **A-corrected** to one mode, close trigger, and next-bar effectiveness.
- **GF-14**
  1. **Type:** bar-driven.
  2. **Config:** `use_break_even: true`, `be_trigger_r: 1.0`, `be_buffer_r: 0.0`, `use_trailing: false`, `use_sl: true`, `use_sl_percent: true`, `sl_percent: 5.0`, `tp_mode: "None"`, `tw_audit_semantics_mode: "off"`, `tw_be_semantics_mode: "local"`.
  3. **Frozen metadata:** `instrument_price_tick 0.01`, `instrument_qty_step 1.0`, `instrument_contract_multiplier 1`; entry quantity `1` via `FIXED_QTY`.
  4. **Bars:** `0 | 2026-01-01T00:00:00Z | 100,100,100,100,1` raw `(1,0)`; `1 | …:01 | 101,106,101,104,1`; `2 | …:02 | 104,105,99,105,1`; `3 | …:03 | 105,105,99,100,1`.
  5. **Expected events:** bar 0 `ENTER_LONG 1 @ 100`, `stop = 95.00`, `initial_R = 5.00`. Bar 1: high `106` reaches `entry + 1R = 105` but the **close** is `104`, so **no arm**; stop stays `95.00`. Bar 2: close `105` reaches 1R, so `BE_ARMED(stop_next_bar=100.00)`; the same bar's low of `99` must **not** produce an exit, because the revision is not yet effective. Bar 3: stop is `100.00`, low `99` crosses it, `EXIT_LONG(reason=BE_STOP)`.
  6. **Expected fills/prices/quantities:** exactly one exit, at `100.00`, on bar 3.
  7. **Expected end state / reasons:** typed reasons `BE_ARMED`, `BE_STOP`; a later candidate revision of `99.00` must be rejected as non-monotonic.
  8. **Discriminator (RED):** run the same bars against A. Under the touch profile A arms on bar 1 from the **high** of `106` (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/exits.py:334-339`); under A's *default* close-only profile (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/config.py:55`; `MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/exits.py:560-561`) it arms on bar 2 from the close of `105`. **In both cases the revision is applied before that same bar's price-exit test** (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/runner.py:551-558`, `:565-567`), so A exits at `100.00` on **bar 2**, whose low is `99`, rather than on bar 3. The fixture records RED at bar 2 under both profiles and GREEN at bar 3, which isolates the same-bar-effectiveness defect independently of the trigger-probe defect.

### C15 — Trailing stop

- **A behaviour:** trailing has priority over BE and takes ownership of the stop; the activation probe and the trail anchor are both the bar **high** for a long unless close-only execution or a legacy `tw_` mode is active; the candidate stop is `anchor − trail_distance_atr_mult × ATR`, adverse-rounded and monotonic — and it is applied on the trigger bar itself (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/exits.py:300-330`, ordering at `MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/runner.py:551-567`).
- **B behaviour:** trails by an **R-multiple** distance rather than an ATR distance, activating from `start_r` and trailing by `dist_r` (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/modules/risk/tp_calculator.py:253-275`; `MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/config/defaults.py:115-121`), recomputed from the running extremes after the price-exit checks (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/engine/mtc_runner.py:2465-2478`), with the trail exit filling at the **bar close** (`:2480-2489`).
- **Pine reference:** ATR-distance, close-triggered trailing (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/01_PINE/MTC_V2.pine:1143-1172`).
- **Disagreement:** the distance definition itself differs (ATR versus R), as do the activation probe and the effective bar.
- **Canonical semantics and reason:** once a **completed close** reaches the configured activation R, compute an ATR-distance candidate from that close, adverse-round it, and activate it on the **next** bar. Revisions are monotonic. Trailing owns the stop while active; BE can neither loosen nor replace it.
- **Chosen implementation:** **A-corrected** for close trigger and next-bar effectiveness; ATR distance retained.
- **GF-15**
  1. **Type:** bar-driven.
  2. **Config:** `use_trailing: true`, `trail_start_r: 1.0`, `trail_distance_atr_mult: 1.5`, `trail_atr_len: 14` with the trail ATR **pinned to 2.0** by fixture injection, `use_break_even: true`, `be_trigger_r: 1.0`, `be_buffer_r: 0.0`, `use_sl: true`, `use_sl_percent: true`, `sl_percent: 5.0`, `tp_mode: "None"`, `tw_audit_semantics_mode: "off"`, `tw_trailing_semantics_mode: "local"`.
  3. **Frozen metadata:** `instrument_price_tick 0.01`, `instrument_qty_step 1.0`, `instrument_contract_multiplier 1`; entry quantity `1` via `FIXED_QTY`.
  4. **Bars:** `0 | 2026-01-01T00:00:00Z | 100,100,100,100,1` raw `(1,0)`; `1 | …:01 | 101,107,101,106,1`; `2 | …:02 | 106,106,102,104,1`; `3 | …:03 | 104,104,102,103,1`.
  5. **Expected events:** bar 0 `ENTER_LONG 1 @ 100`, `stop 95.00`, `initial_R 5.00`. Bar 1 close `106` reaches `entry + 1R = 105`: `TRAIL_ARMED(candidate = 106 − 1.5 × 2.0 = 103.00, effective_next_bar)`; the same bar produces no exit. Bar 2: the trail stop is `103.00` and the bar low is `102`, so `EXIT_LONG(reason=TRAIL_STOP)`. In a variant that deletes bar 2 and keeps only bar 3 (`104,104,102,103`), the later close of `103` produces a candidate of `103 − 3 = 100.00`, which is **below** the standing `103.00` and must be rejected as non-monotonic.
  6. **Expected fills/prices/quantities:** exit fill `103.00` on bar 2; the trigger-bar low of `101` on bar 1 must not fill at `103.00`.
  7. **Expected end state / reasons:** typed reasons `TRAIL_ARMED`, `TRAIL_STOP`, `TRAIL_MONOTONIC_REJECTED`; while trailing is active no `BE_ARMED` event may appear.
  8. **Discriminator (RED):** three REDs. (i) A with defaults arms from bar 1's **high** of 107, giving a candidate of `104.00` effective on bar 1 itself, so A exits on **bar 1** at `104.00` (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/exits.py:302-328`). (ii) B replaces the ATR distance with `dist_r × initial_R = 2.0 × 5.00 = 10.00`, giving a trail stop of `96.00` and no exit at all in these four bars (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/modules/risk/tp_calculator.py:253-275`). (iii) B fills a trail exit at the **bar close** rather than the trail price (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/engine/mtc_runner.py:2480-2489`).

### C16 — Opposite-signal and filter exits

- **A behaviour:** the per-bar exit order is fixed: protective stop-owner update, then price exits, then opposite-signal exit, then filter-block exits, then time exits (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/runner.py:551-558`, `:565-590`, `:592-605`, `:609-665`, `:667-704`). The filter-exit branch is a nine-way ladder evaluated in a single fixed order — MA, MA-slope, McGinley, volume, ATR-volatility, range (ADX **or** Chop), candle pattern, level proximity, HTF trend — with the **first** match winning (`:620-656`). There is no master toggle; A's configuration records that the master gate is deliberately not enforced (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/config.py:131-135`).
- **B behaviour:** exposes a single master `exit_on_filter_block` plus a seven-field per-filter block (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/config/defaults.py:271-280`, `:290`), resolved in its own helper (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/engine/mtc_runner.py:400-437`) and applied inside the exit pipeline (`:2432-2448`).
- **Pine reference:** opposite and filter exits at `MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/01_PINE/MTC_V2.pine:1468-1542`.
- **Disagreement:** B's master switch can enable exit behaviour that A and Pine do not have, and the two ladders do not share an order, so the **reason** attached to the same bar differs even when the exit does not.
- **Canonical semantics and reason:** protective fills win first. If none fires, a valid opposite signal exits next, then individually enabled filter exits in a frozen documented order. There is no hidden master fallback: a disabled filter never fires. One bar emits at most one full-exit reason.
- **Chosen implementation:** **A**, with A's nine-branch order adopted verbatim as the frozen documented order.
- **GF-16**
  1. **Type:** bar-driven.
  2. **Config:** four runs over one bar. Common: `use_sl: true`, `use_sl_percent: true`, `sl_percent: 5.0`, `tp_mode: "None"`, `exit_on_opposite_signal: true`, `exit_on_ma_block: true`, `exit_on_htf_trend_block: true`, all other `exit_on_*` false. R1 all three triggers present; R2 removes the stop (`sl_percent: 20.0`); R3 removes the stop and the opposite signal; R4 sets `exit_on_ma_block: false` and additionally sets a legacy master-like field `exit_on_filter_block: true`.
  3. **Frozen metadata:** `instrument_price_tick 0.01`, `instrument_qty_step 1.0`, `instrument_contract_multiplier 1`.
  4. **Bars:** `0 | 2026-01-01T00:00:00Z | 100,100,100,100,1` raw `(1,0)`; `1 | …:01 | 100,100,94,96,1` raw `(0,1)`, with the MA gate injected `long_ok=false` and the HTF-trend gate injected `long_ok=false`.
  5. **Expected events:** R1 — `EXIT_LONG(reason=STOP)` only. R2 — `EXIT_LONG(reason=OPP_SIGNAL)` only. R3 — `EXIT_LONG(reason=FILTER_BLOCK, gate=ma_filter)` only, because MA precedes HTF trend in the frozen order. R4 — `EXIT_LONG(reason=FILTER_BLOCK, gate=htf_trend_filter)`; the `exit_on_filter_block: true` field must not re-enable the disabled MA exit.
  6. **Expected fills/prices/quantities:** R1 fill `95.00`; R2, R3, R4 fill at the decision close `96.00`.
  7. **Expected end state / reasons:** exactly one full-exit reason per bar in every run.
  8. **Discriminator (RED):** R4 against B, whose master `exit_on_filter_block` participates in the resolution (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/config/defaults.py:290`; `MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/engine/mtc_runner.py:400-437`) and can therefore produce an MA-attributed exit that the decided rule forbids. A second RED reorders the nine-branch ladder so R3 reports `htf_trend_filter`.

### C17 — Bar/time/PnL exits

- **A behaviour:** the bar counter is `bar.bar_index − entry_bar` (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/runner.py:673-674`), but the `Profit Only` / `Loss Only` condition reads `state.last_realized_pnl` — the **previous closed trade**, not the open position (`:677-685`). EOD fires on the **first bar of a new calendar day**, keyed on the bar's own UTC timestamp string (`:687-691`). EOW is hard-coded to "Friday and hour ≥ 21 UTC" (`:692-695`).
- **B behaviour:** the same bar counter, but the condition reads the open position's **unrealised** PnL with strict comparisons (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/engine/mtc_runner.py:479-504`). EOD/EOW also fire on the first bar of a new day / new ISO week (`:440-455`, `:495-496`), and the config carries a selectable calendar timezone (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/config/defaults.py:256-268`). B additionally defines true end-of-day / end-of-week predicates that are not used by the time-stop path (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/engine/mtc_runner.py:457-477`).
- **Pine reference:** open-trade PnL with day/week boundaries (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/01_PINE/MTC_V2.pine:1544-1605`).
- **Disagreement:** A reads the wrong PnL entirely — a profitable open position can be closed under `Loss Only` because the previous trade lost. Separately, both engines exit at the **open of the next period** while the label says "end of day"; and A's Friday-21:00 rule is a hard-coded venue assumption. Corpus cases 110 and 111 record all three pairwise strict comparisons failing with identical trade counts of 142 and 136, i.e. the divergence is timing and price, not selection (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/12_PARITY_PINETS/parity_summary.md:70-76`, `:78-84`).
- **Canonical semantics and reason:** `Bars` counts completed bars since the fill. `Profit Only` / `Loss Only` examine the **current unrealised PnL of the open position** at the decision close. EOD/EOW exits occur at the **last scheduled bar close** of the frozen venue/session calendar, not at the first bar of the next period; for 24/7 profiles day and week are UTC. A missing calendar **blocks** those modes with a typed reason rather than falling back to a hard-coded hour.
- **Chosen implementation:** **A-corrected**, adopting Pine's and B's open-position PnL concept and C25's time discipline.
- **GF-17**
  1. **Type:** bar-driven.
  2. **Config:** run R1 `{use_time_stop: true, time_stop_bars: 3, time_stop_condition: "Always"}`; run R2 `{use_time_stop: true, time_stop_bars: 999, time_stop_condition: "Loss Only", loss_threshold: -5.0}`; run R3 `{time_stop_eod: true, venue_calendar: "FROZEN_TEST_CAL_24x5"}`; run R4 the same as R3 with `venue_calendar: null`. All runs `use_sl: false`, `tp_mode: "None"`.
  3. **Frozen metadata:** `FROZEN_TEST_CAL_24x5` declares the last scheduled bar of Friday 2026-01-02 as the 21:45Z bar and the first bar of Monday 2026-01-05 as 00:00Z; `instrument_contract_multiplier 1`, `instrument_qty_step 1.0`.
  4. **Bars:** R1 — fill on bar 5 at `100`, bars 6, 7, 8 all `100,100,100,100,1`. R2 — a prior closed trade with realised PnL `+100`, then an open long filled at `100` with the decision bar `1 | 100,100,90,90,1` giving unrealised `−10`. R3/R4 — `… | 2026-01-02T21:30:00Z | 100,100,100,100,1`, `… | 2026-01-02T21:45:00Z | 100,100,100,100,1`, `… | 2026-01-05T00:00:00Z | 100,100,100,100,1`.
  5. **Expected events:** R1 — `EXIT(reason=TIME_STOP_BARS)` at bar 8 close (`8 − 5 = 3`). R2 — `EXIT(reason=TIME_STOP_LOSS)` because current unrealised `−10 <= −5`; the prior realised `+100` must not suppress it. R3 — `EXIT(reason=TIME_STOP_EOD)` on the **21:45Z Friday bar**, the last scheduled bar of that calendar day. R4 — `BLOCK(reason=CALENDAR_UNAVAILABLE)`; no time exit on any bar.
  6. **Expected fills/prices/quantities:** every time exit fills at the decision close of its own bar.
  7. **Expected end state / reasons:** typed reasons `TIME_STOP_BARS`, `TIME_STOP_LOSS`, `TIME_STOP_EOD`, `CALENDAR_UNAVAILABLE`.
  8. **Discriminator (RED):** R2 against A, which reads `last_realized_pnl = +100` and therefore does **not** exit under `Loss Only` (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/runner.py:677-685`). R3 against both A and B, which exit on the **2026-01-05T00:00:00Z** bar instead of the Friday 21:45Z bar (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/runner.py:687-691`; `MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/engine/mtc_runner.py:440-446`, `:495`). R4 against A, whose EOW branch fires from a hard-coded Friday-21:00 rule with no calendar at all (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/runner.py:692-695`).

### C18 — Session/day limits, guards, and recovery

- **A behaviour:** guards are evaluated in the order daily-loss → max-trades → max-drawdown → consecutive-loss → equity-curve → MAE (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/runner.py:762-794`), and post-exit cooldown is a separate later term (`:813-820`). Three of those guards use inputs that differ from B's: the day trade counter increments on **any bar with a close reason**, i.e. it counts closes rather than entries (`:746-747`); the equity-curve SMA is built only from realised equity **at closes** (`:749-756`); and the MAE guard tests `_l16_mae_last`, the MAE of the **previous closed trade** (`:724-727`, `:789-792`). Guard recovery has two modes, and the `Bars` countdown runs unconditionally once armed (`:796-811`). Session eligibility uses named sessions with hard-coded IANA zones and passes through an unknown name (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/gates.py:514-519`, `:588-598`).
- **B behaviour:** the order is drawdown → consecutive-loss → cooldown → equity-curve → daily-loss → max-trades → MAE (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/engine/mtc_runner.py:333-380`); the day counter is `_daily_entry_count`, incremented on entries and reset at the day roll (`:254-266`, `:373-376`); the equity-curve guard uses mark-to-market equity including the current bar (`:346-368`); the MAE guard uses the **current open position's** MAE (`:296-307`, `:377-380`); and recovery has four modes (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/config/defaults.py:249-253`).
- **Pine reference:** session gating and guards at `MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/01_PINE/MTC_V2.pine:1056-1087`, `:1607-1681`.
- **Disagreement:** the guard **order**, the counted **event**, and the measured **quantity** all differ, so the same bar can block for different reasons or not at all. Separately, account-wide facts cannot be authoritative inside one strategy worker; WP-P0-04 already defines the Guardian veto vocabulary (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/contracts/mtc_contracts/risk.py:17-24`, `:35-109`).
- **Canonical semantics and reason:** the kernel owns strategy-local max-trades-per-day (counting **entries**), entry spacing and post-exit cooldown (C06), session eligibility, equity-curve and MAE signal guards, and the declared recovery mode. Portfolio and account daily loss, drawdown, consecutive-loss, exposure and kill vetoes belong to the PortfolioSimulator/Guardian. Session calendars use authoritative venue timestamps and frozen **named** calendars — never a host locale and never a silent pass-through for an unknown name. A veto blocks entries and adds; it never suppresses a protective exit.
- **Chosen implementation:** **NEW ownership split**, with A's guard order adopted as the local baseline and the counted event corrected to entries.
- **GF-18**
  1. **Type:** bar-driven.
  2. **Config:** `use_max_trades_per_day: true`, `max_trades_per_day: 2`, `use_mae_guard: false`, `use_guard_recovery: false`, `use_sl: true`, `use_sl_percent: true`, `sl_percent: 5.0`, `tp_mode: "None"`, `session_calendar: "FROZEN_TEST_CAL_24x7_UTC"`. Run R2 injects a Guardian veto record `{veto: true, reason: "BUCKET_LOSS_BUDGET"}` on bar 3. Run R3 repeats the whole run with the host TZ set to `UTC` and then to `America/New_York`.
  3. **Frozen metadata:** `FROZEN_TEST_CAL_24x7_UTC` defines the canonical day boundary at 00:00Z; `instrument_qty_step 1.0`, `instrument_contract_multiplier 1`.
  4. **Bars:** `0 | 2026-01-01T22:00:00Z | 100,100,100,100,1` raw `(1,0)`; `1 | …T22:30:00Z | 100,100,94,96,1` raw `(0,0)` (stop exit); `2 | …T23:00:00Z | 96,96,96,96,1` raw `(1,0)`; `3 | …T23:30:00Z | 96,96,96,96,1` raw `(1,0)`; `4 | 2026-01-02T00:00:00Z | 96,96,96,96,1` raw `(1,0)`.
  5. **Expected events:** bar 0 `ENTER_LONG` (entry 1 of the day); bar 1 `EXIT_LONG(reason=STOP)`; bar 2 `ENTER_LONG` (entry 2); bar 3 `BLOCK(reason=MAX_TRADES_PER_DAY)`; bar 4 `ENTER_LONG`, because the canonical day rolled. R2 — bar 3 blocks with `GUARDIAN_VETO:BUCKET_LOSS_BUDGET`, and the stop exit at bar 1 still executes under an injected veto. R3 — both host-timezone runs produce byte-identical event streams.

A second RED reorders the guard ladder to B's order (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/engine/mtc_runner.py:333-380`) and asserts that the reported first blocking reason changes.

### C19 — Same-bar stop/target collision

**A content claim in the previous revision was false and is corrected here.** A does **not** expose a selectable collision policy; it hard-codes stop-first.

- **A behaviour:** when both a stop and a target are hit on the same bar, `evaluate_price_exit` returns the **stop** result unconditionally and stamps `is_pessimistic=True`; there is no policy field, no configuration key and no alternative branch (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/exits.py:362-379`).
- **B behaviour:** the production runner infers an intrabar path from the bar geometry — `_tv_high_first` returns `True` when `|open − high| <= |open − low|`, so a tie resolves **high-first** — and then lets the target win for a long (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/engine/mtc_runner.py:612-619`, `:2236-2258`). Separately, B's legacy `fills` module declares a four-value `SameBarConflictPolicy` (`WORST_CASE`, `BEST_CASE`, `SL_PRIORITY`, `TP_PRIORITY`) and a `resolve_same_bar_conflict` helper (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/engine/fills.py:36-47`, `:172-220`); that module has **no importer in the production tree** and is referenced only by one test (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/tests/test_fill_contract_baseline.py:3-6`). It is a legacy surface, not the live policy, and it is dispositioned in `MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/11_TRIAGE/WP_P0_09_CAPABILITY_TABLE_2026-08-25/COVERAGE_SWEEP.md` §7.
- **Pine reference:** Pine also hard-codes stop-first and sets a pessimistic flag (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/01_PINE/MTC_V2.pine:1305-1310`).
- **Disagreement:** OHLC cannot prove intrabar order. A and Pine assume the adverse outcome silently; B guesses a path from bar geometry and can therefore select the favourable outcome on exactly the bars where the question matters.
- **Canonical semantics and reason:** every run **records** one of `STOP_FIRST`, `TARGET_FIRST`, `SUBBAR_UNKNOWN`. The acceptance default is `STOP_FIRST`. `SUBBAR_UNKNOWN` yields an unresolved or interval result with reason `AMBIGUOUS_INTRABAR_ORDER` unless sub-bar evidence is supplied; it must never guess a path. Reason: the pessimistic assumption is defensible, but only if it is a **declared** policy that shows up in the artifact; an undeclared assumption cannot be audited or varied.
- **Chosen implementation:** **A-corrected** — A's stop-first outcome is retained as the default, and the missing explicit policy field and reason code are added.
- **GF-19**
  1. **Type:** bar-driven.
  2. **Config:** three runs, `collision_policy` set to `STOP_FIRST`, `TARGET_FIRST`, `SUBBAR_UNKNOWN`; `use_sl: true`, `use_sl_percent: true`, `sl_percent: 5.0`, `tp_mode: "Percent"`, `tp_percent: 5.0`, execution per C20 touch semantics.
  3. **Frozen metadata:** `instrument_price_tick 0.01`, `instrument_qty_step 1.0`, `instrument_contract_multiplier 1`; entry quantity `1` via `FIXED_QTY`.
  4. **Bars:** `0 | 2026-01-01T00:00:00Z | 100,100,100,100,1` raw `(1,0)`; `1 | …:01 | 100,106,94,101,1`.
  5. **Expected events:** the entry sets `stop = 95.00` and `target = 105.00`; bar 1 touches both. `STOP_FIRST` → `EXIT_LONG(reason=STOP)`. `TARGET_FIRST` → `EXIT_LONG(reason=TP_PERCENT)`. `SUBBAR_UNKNOWN` → `UNRESOLVED(reason=AMBIGUOUS_INTRABAR_ORDER)` with no scalar PnL and the position still open for reporting purposes.
  6. **Expected fills/prices/quantities:** `95.00`, `105.00`, and none respectively.
  7. **Expected end state / reasons:** the policy value appears in the run artifact in all three cases; the short mirror uses `O=100,H=106,L=94,C=99` with `stop 105.00` / `target 95.00`.
  8. **Discriminator (RED):** the chosen bar is a deliberate tie — `|100 − 106| = 6` and `|100 − 94| = 6` — so B's `d_high <= d_low` test returns `True`, chooses high-first, and therefore fills the **target at 105.00** where the decided default fills the **stop at 95.00** (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/engine/mtc_runner.py:612-619`, `:2236-2258`). The `STOP_FIRST` run must fail RED against that heuristic. A second RED asserts that A cannot express the `TARGET_FIRST` or `SUBBAR_UNKNOWN` rows at all, because it has no policy field (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/exits.py:362-379`).

### C20 — Fill assumptions and gaps

- **A behaviour:** two execution profiles exist, `raw_close_only_v1` and `close_only_deterministic_v2`, and the **default is close-only** (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/config.py:10-14`, `:55`; profile test at `MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/exits.py:560-561`). In the non-close-only path a stop fills at the **open** when the open is already beyond it and at the stop price on a touch (`:395-421`), and a target fills at the **open** on a favourable gap and at the target price on a touch (`:450-493`). In close-only mode both fill at the bar close (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/exits.py:424-447`, `MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/exits.py:496-540`).
- **B behaviour:** a `fill_contract` of `touch` or `close` (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/config/defaults.py:358-361`), the inferred intrabar path of C19, and a tick slippage applied to every fill (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/engine/mtc_runner.py:621-629`).
- **Pine reference:** the strategy is declared `process_orders_on_close=true` (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/01_PINE/MTC_V2.pine:7`) and implements close-only fills (`:1174-1348`).
- **Disagreement:** Pine-compatible close-only fills are not faithful to a native standing protective order, and A ships close-only as the **default** rather than as a named legacy profile.
- **Canonical semantics and reason:** canonical execution uses standing touch semantics — a stop gap fills at the worse open, a target gap at the better open, otherwise at the order price. Market decisions fill at the declared decision close unless venue execution policy says next open. `LEGACY_CLOSE_ONLY` remains a **named** profile and is never an implicit default.
- **Chosen implementation:** **A** touch semantics as canonical; A's close-only becomes a named legacy fixture profile.
- **GF-20** — eight canonical rows plus one legacy row, all defined here so the fixture references only rows that exist.
  1. **Type:** bar-driven.
  2. **Config:** rows R1–R8 use `execution_profile: "STANDING_TOUCH"`; row R9 uses `execution_profile: "LEGACY_CLOSE_ONLY"`. All rows `use_sl: true`, `use_sl_percent: true`, `sl_percent: 5.0`, `tp_mode: "Percent"`, `tp_percent: 5.0`, slippage `0`, fees `0`.
  3. **Frozen metadata:** `instrument_price_tick 0.01`, `instrument_qty_step 1.0`, `instrument_contract_multiplier 1`; entry quantity `1` via `FIXED_QTY`.
  4. **Bars:** every row is entry bar `0 | 2026-01-01T00:00:00Z | 100,100,100,100,1` followed by one test bar:

     | Row | Side | Stop | Target | Test bar `O,H,L,C` | Case |
     |---|---|---|---|---|---|
     | R1 | LONG | 95.00 | 105.00 | `99,99,94,96` | stop, normal touch |
     | R2 | LONG | 95.00 | 105.00 | `90,92,88,91` | stop, adverse gap |
     | R3 | LONG | 95.00 | 105.00 | `101,106,100,104` | target, normal touch |
     | R4 | LONG | 95.00 | 105.00 | `109,110,108,110` | target, favourable gap |
     | R5 | SHORT | 105.00 | 95.00 | `101,106,101,104` | stop, normal touch |
     | R6 | SHORT | 105.00 | 95.00 | `110,112,108,109` | stop, adverse gap |
     | R7 | SHORT | 105.00 | 95.00 | `99,100,94,96` | target, normal touch |
     | R8 | SHORT | 105.00 | 95.00 | `91,92,90,90` | target, favourable gap |
     | R9 | LONG | 95.00 | 105.00 | `99,99,94,96` | legacy close-only, same crossed stop |

  5. **Expected events:** one exit per row — `STOP` for R1, R2, R5, R6; `TP_PERCENT` for R3, R4, R7, R8; `STOP` for R9 labelled `profile=LEGACY_CLOSE_ONLY, canonical=false`.
  6. **Expected fills/prices/quantities:** R1 `95.00`; R2 `90.00`; R3 `105.00`; R4 `109.00`; R5 `105.00`; R6 `110.00`; R7 `95.00`; R8 `91.00`; R9 `96.00`. **Every one of the four gap rows fills at its test bar's open, and in every one of them the open differs from the close**, so no row's expected value coincides with the close-only rival's.

Rival fills, stated exactly: R2 `91.00` against the canonical `90.00`; R6 `109.00` against `110.00`; R4 `110.00` against `109.00`; R8 `90.00` against `91.00`. Each pair differs, so all four rows discriminate as specified and none needs to be re-run or re-chosen.

### C21 — Same-bar re-entry after exits

- **A behaviour:** a price exit sets `entry_blocked_by_exit` for the bar (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/runner.py:586-590`), as do filter-block and time exits (`:657-665`, `:696-704`); an opposite-signal exit blocks only when `allow_flip` is false (`:592-605`). Admission permits a same-bar re-entry **only** when the bar's close reason was the opposite-signal exit (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/position_manager.py:119-121`, `:137-142`). Legacy `tw_` modes can additionally queue a protective-exit re-entry (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/runner.py:1293-1332`).
- **B behaviour:** exposes `allow_same_bar_reentry` (default **true**), `same_bar_reentry_requires_exit` and `same_bar_reentry_max_per_bar` (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/config/defaults.py:299-314`), refusing only a `max_per_bar` other than 1 (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/engine/mtc_runner.py:146-147`); re-entry after an exit re-evaluates guards on the same bar (`:1630-1646`).
- **Pine reference:** exit ordering precedes entry logic (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/01_PINE/MTC_V2.pine:1174-1820`).
- **Disagreement:** B's default permits a new entry on the same bar as **any** exit, including a stop; A permits it only after an opposite-signal exit; the legacy `tw_` modes add a third behaviour.
- **Canonical semantics and reason:** a stop, target, margin, filter, time or Guardian exit blocks **all** new entries for that bar. Only an opposite-signal full exit may reverse at the same close, and only under C05 with a valid post-gate opposite decision. Reason: allowing a risk exit to be followed immediately by a new position on the same bar converts a protective event into a re-risking event with no new information.
- **Chosen implementation:** **A-corrected**, removing the legacy protective re-entry modes.
- **GF-21**
  1. **Type:** bar-driven.
  2. **Config:** four runs. R1 `{use_sl: true, use_sl_percent: true, sl_percent: 5.0, tp_mode: "None"}`; R2 `{tp_mode: "Percent", tp_percent: 5.0, use_sl: false}`; R3 `{allow_same_close_reversal: true, exit_on_opposite_signal: true}`; R4 the same as R3 with `allow_same_close_reversal: false`.
  3. **Frozen metadata:** `instrument_price_tick 0.01`, `instrument_qty_step 1.0`, `instrument_contract_multiplier 1`; entry quantity `1` via `FIXED_QTY`.
  4. **Bars:** `0 | 2026-01-01T00:00:00Z | 100,100,100,100,1` raw `(1,0)`; then R1 `1 | …:01 | 99,99,94,96,1` raw `(1,0)` (stop plus a fresh same-direction signal); R2 `1 | …:01 | 101,106,101,104,1` raw `(0,1)` (target plus opposite signal); R3 and R4 `1 | …:01 | 96,96,95,95,1` raw `(0,1)` (no protective hit, opposite signal only).
  5. **Expected events:** R1 — `EXIT_LONG(reason=STOP)` only; no entry on bar 1. R2 — `EXIT_LONG(reason=TP_PERCENT)` only; no short entry on bar 1. R3 — `EXIT_LONG(reason=OPP_SIGNAL)` then `ENTER_SHORT`, in that order, with distinct intent IDs. R4 — `EXIT_LONG(reason=OPP_SIGNAL)` only.
  6. **Expected fills/prices/quantities:** R1 exit `95.00`; R2 exit `105.00`; R3 exit `95.00` and entry `95.00`; R4 exit `95.00`.
  7. **Expected end state / reasons:** typed reason `SAME_BAR_REENTRY_BLOCKED` accompanies the suppressed entry in R1 and R2.
  8. **Discriminator (RED):** R1 against B with its defaults, where `allow_same_bar_reentry: true` and `same_bar_reentry_requires_exit: true` are both satisfied by the stop exit and a second entry is attempted on the same bar (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/config/defaults.py:303-310`; `MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/engine/mtc_runner.py:1630-1646`). A second RED runs R1 against A with `tw_audit_semantics_mode: "research"` and `tw_reversal_reentry_mode: "carry_to_next_bar_after_protective_exit"`, which queues the suppressed entry for a later bar (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/runner.py:1293-1308`).

### C22 — Fees, slippage, and funding

- **A behaviour:** position PnL applies the contract multiplier but there is **no fee, slippage or funding ledger anywhere in A** (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/position_manager.py:254-355`; `MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/runner.py:1504-1525`). A's configuration declares no commission, slippage or funding key (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/config.py:28-246`).
- **B behaviour:** defaults commission to `0.04`% and slippage to five ticks (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/config/defaults.py:334-340`), applies the slippage to every fill (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/engine/mtc_runner.py:621-629`), and deducts commission at both ends (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/engine/mtc_state.py:349-437`). Its dedicated cost module `engine/fee_model.py` — one of the modules the audit required be swept — computes commission on the **slippage-adjusted** price and charges slippage adversely on both sides (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/engine/fee_model.py:149-191`, `:193-247`), but it computes `notional = price × quantity` with **no contract multiplier** (`:122-124`), declares a `tick_size_source` selector that `from_config` never reads (`:42-45`, `:100-107`), models **no funding at all**, and **has no importer anywhere in the production tree** — `engine/__init__.py` exports neither it nor `fills` (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/engine/__init__.py:3-17`). It is dispositioned as a legacy non-authority in `MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/11_TRIAGE/WP_P0_09_CAPABILITY_TABLE_2026-08-25/COVERAGE_SWEEP.md` §7.
- **Pine reference:** the strategy declaration sets no commission, slippage or funding (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/01_PINE/MTC_V2.pine:7`).
- **Disagreement:** A and Pine effectively omit costs; B invents defaults; none models scheduled funding.
- **Canonical semantics and reason:** every run binds a frozen fee schedule (maker/taker plus currency), a slippage model and a funding schedule, or explicitly declares `ZERO_COST_RESEARCH`. Fees accrue **per execution fragment**, slippage changes the fill price, and funding accrues at venue timestamps while the position is held. Production evidence may not silently use zero, and absence of a schedule is `COST_MODEL_UNAVAILABLE`, not free trading.
- **Chosen implementation:** **NEW** explicit cost contract; B's `fee_model` arithmetic is an arithmetic reference only, corrected by C08's multiplier.
- **GF-22**
  1. **Type:** bar-driven.
  2. **Config:** run R1 `{fee_schedule: {taker_rate: 0.001, maker_rate: 0.001, currency: "USDT"}, slippage_model: {ticks: 1, direction: "ADVERSE"}, funding_schedule: [{ts: "2026-01-01T00:30:00Z", rate: 0.0001, side_sign: "LONG_PAYS"}]}`; run R2 identical but with `funding_schedule: null` and `cost_declaration` unset; run R3 identical to R2 but with `cost_declaration: "ZERO_COST_RESEARCH"`.
  3. **Frozen metadata:** `instrument_price_tick 0.50`, `instrument_qty_step 1.0`, `instrument_contract_multiplier 1`; entry quantity `2` via `FIXED_QTY`.
  4. **Bars:** `0 | 2026-01-01T00:00:00Z | 100,100,100,100,1` raw `(1,0)`; `1 | 2026-01-01T00:30:00Z | 100,100,100,100,1` raw `(0,0)`; `2 | 2026-01-01T01:00:00Z | 110,110,110,110,1` raw `(0,1)`.
  5. **Expected events:** R1 — `FILL(BUY)` at bar 0, `FUNDING` at bar 1, `FILL(SELL)` at bar 2, `LIFECYCLE_CLOSED`. R2 — `COST_MODEL_UNAVAILABLE` at run start and no fills. R3 — fills with a zero fee/slippage ledger, artifact labelled `ZERO_COST_RESEARCH`.
  6. **Expected fills/prices/quantities:** R1 — buy fill `100.50` (nominal 100 plus one 0.50 tick adverse), sell fill `109.50`; entry fee `100.50 × 2 × 0.001 = 0.201`; exit fee `109.50 × 2 × 0.001 = 0.219`; gross PnL `(109.50 − 100.50) × 2 × 1 = 18.00`; exactly one funding item, sign negative for a long payer, magnitude `100.50 × 2 × 0.0001 = 0.0201`; net PnL `18.00 − 0.201 − 0.219 − 0.0201 = 17.5599`.
  7. **Expected end state / reasons:** typed reasons `COST_MODEL_UNAVAILABLE`, `ZERO_COST_RESEARCH`; the ledger lists one fee item per fragment, never one per lifecycle.
  8. **Discriminator (RED):** R2 against A, which runs to completion with zero cost and no declaration at all (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/config.py:28-246`), and against B, which substitutes its `0.04`% / five-tick defaults (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/config/defaults.py:334-335`). A second RED sets `contract_multiplier: 10` and asserts the fee becomes `100.50 × 2 × 10 × 0.001 = 2.01`; B's `fee_model` returns `0.201` because its notional omits the multiplier (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/engine/fee_model.py:122-124`).

### C23 — Warm-up, evaluation boundary, and terminal handling

- **A behaviour:** warm-up is the **exact maximum** of the declared dependency requirements (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/runner.py:304-312`), and entries are blocked while `bar.bar_index < warmup_bars - 1` or the signal indicator is not warm (`:451-459`). There is no pre-roll concept, no evaluation window and no terminal action.
- **B behaviour:** warm-up is an **estimate**: non-RMA lookbacks are taken raw, RMA-based lookbacks are multiplied by `_RMA_CONVERGENCE_MULT = 5`, and the result is clamped to `[_WARMUP_FLOOR = 200, _WARMUP_CEIL = 2000]` (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/engine/mtc_runner.py:511-513`, `:515-579`). Pre-roll, evaluation-boundary and terminal behaviour are configuration: `preroll_days`, `preroll_mode` (`warmup_only` or `trade`), `close_open_at_eval_start` (default **true**) and `force_terminal_manual_close` (default **true**) (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/config/defaults.py:367-403`), plus `close_open_at_end` (`:342-348`).
- **Pine reference:** relies on series readiness plus its own readiness inputs (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/01_PINE/MTC_V2.pine:943-1119`).
- **Disagreement:** a clamp changes the first actionable bar by up to 2000 bars, and two of B's boundary defaults **silently flatten** a position — one at the evaluation start and one at the end. Corpus B's two unresolved cases both pass only after overlap clipping and are tagged `TV_EARLY_TRADE_END_CANDIDATE`, with the raw-count-versus-overlap policy explicitly unresolved (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/parity_suite_350/PARITY_STATUS_FINAL_20260304.md:167-173`, `:187-200`) — that unresolved policy is this row.
- **Canonical semantics and reason:** warm-up is the exact maximum dependency requirement of the frozen indicator graph plus explicitly supplied pre-roll. The first actionable bar must have every enabled dependency ready. Evaluation start, evaluation end and terminal action are **scenario fields**; no position is silently flattened, and no trade set is silently clipped for comparison. A comparison that clips must record the clip as a declared transformation.
- **Chosen implementation:** **NEW** explicit scenario contract, using A's dependency accounting.

  2. **Config:** run R1 a profile whose maximum declared dependency is exactly 20 completed bars, `preroll_bars: 0`; run R2 the same profile with `preroll_bars: 20`; run R3 `{terminal_action: "NONE"}`; run R4 `{terminal_action: "FLATTEN"}`; run R5 `{eval_start_action: "NONE"}` with a position open at the evaluation start.

R2 — with 20 pre-roll bars supplied ahead of evaluation bar 0, evaluation bar 0 is actionable. R3 — the position open at bar 24 remains open; the artifact records `terminal_open_position=true`. R4 — exactly one labelled `TERMINAL_FLATTEN` fill at bar 24 close. R5 — the position open at the evaluation start is **not** closed and no `EVAL_START_FLATTEN` event appears.

R5 against B's defaults, where `close_open_at_eval_start: true` emits an `EVAL_START_FLATTEN` the decided rule forbids (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/config/defaults.py:387-394`), and R3 against `force_terminal_manual_close: true` (`:395-403`).

### C24 — Invalid values, equality boundaries, and short symmetry

- **A behaviour:** a bar is rejected when any price is non-finite or the OHLC relations are violated (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/runner.py:1565-1575`); the sizer returns a bare `0.0` for a non-finite or non-positive entry or equity, with **no typed reason** (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/position_sizer.py:35-38`, `:57-58`); rounding helpers raise on a non-positive tick or step (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/rounding.py:36-40`); and gate thresholds mix strict and non-strict comparisons — for example `>=` for the volume, ADX and ATR-floor gates and `<` for the Chop gate (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/gates.py:101`, `:120`, `:168`, `:143`).
- **B behaviour:** a NaN indicator becomes a fabricated constant rather than a rejection (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/modules/risk/sl_calculator.py:94-95`, `:152-153`; `MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/modules/risk/tp_calculator.py:132-133`), and the stop distance is floored to `max(0.1% of entry, 10 × mintick)` so a degenerate stop silently becomes a valid one (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/modules/risk/position_sizer.py:64-70`).
- **Pine reference:** Pine's `na` propagation differs from Python's, and the shared arithmetic helpers are `MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/01_PINE/MTC_V2.pine:240-361`.
- **Disagreement:** NaN, zero and exact-threshold cases pass, fall back, or round differently in each implementation, and none of them is a **typed** outcome.
- **Canonical semantics and reason:** a non-finite or non-positive price, equity, multiplier, tick, step or required distance **fails closed with a typed reason**. Numeric policy thresholds are **open** — a value exactly at the threshold does not cross — unless a capability explicitly states otherwise, matching WP-P0-04's unset-is-fail-closed rule (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/contracts/README.md:36-38`). Every long fixture has a price-reflected short twin; any permitted asymmetry must be declared, not accidental.
- **Chosen implementation:** **A-corrected**, adding typed reasons and one boundary convention.

Boundary run: `use_adx_filter: true`, `adx_threshold: 25.0`, with the ADX value injected as exactly `25.0` and then as `25.000001`.

Boundary run: ADX exactly `25.0` **does not cross** the open threshold and yields `BLOCK(reason="adx_filter")`; `25.000001` yields `PASS`.

The `NaN` cells against A, which return `0.0` with no reason (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/position_sizer.py:35-38`). ### C25 — Timestamp discipline (ticket #45; incorporated, not re-decided)

- **A behaviour:** `Bar` carries a timestamp and a bar index but **no venue, symbol or timeframe identity** (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/types.py:11-20`). Session membership is computed by converting the bar timestamp into a hard-coded IANA zone per named session (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/gates.py:514-519`, `:524-544`), and the daily/weekly HTF resample accepts only `UTC±offset` chart-timezone labels — a named zone such as `Europe/London` silently degrades to UTC (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/htf.py:75-96`).
- **B behaviour:** converts input timestamps to UTC but exposes a configurable calendar timezone for the time-stop path (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/config/defaults.py:265-268`; `MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/engine/mtc_runner.py:440-455`). Its own corpus cases declare `tv_tz: "Europe/London"` (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/parity_suite_350/cases/parity_bnd_211_swing_right_bars_v03.json:3`).
- **Pine reference:** evaluates exchange/chart series time and named session inputs (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/01_PINE/MTC_V2.pine:1056-1087`, `:1544-1605`).
- **Disagreement:** a local timezone, a DST transition or a display-time setting can leak into economic state, and A's silent UTC degradation for a named chart timezone is exactly that leak in the opposite direction.
- **Canonical semantics and reason:** incorporated verbatim from the already-decided WP-P0-09 row — venue candle timestamps are authoritative; internal timestamps are UTC; local timezone and DST never enter ledger, artifact or hash state; timezone is display-only; the host NTP/drift mechanism is WP-P0-26; the local clock is used only to compute staleness relative to the venue timestamp (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/11_TRIAGE/MASTER_WORK_PACKAGE_AND_PARALLEL_DELIVERY_PLAN_2026-08-22.md:378`; `MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/11_TRIAGE/WAYFINDER_DECISION_FOLD_2026-08-23.md:22`). This row adds nothing to that decision; it only supplies the fixture.
- **Chosen implementation:** **ticket #45 decision**, unchanged.
