### C26 — Duplicate/reordered bars, cancel/revision ordering, and idempotence

- **A behaviour:** the runner iterates whatever bar sequence it is handed, in order, with no identity check, no duplicate detection and no revision protocol (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/runner.py:314-350`); bar identity is a timestamp plus an index only (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/types.py:11-20`).
- **B behaviour:** converts and iterates a dataframe with the same absence of a cross-process idempotency contract (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/engine/mtc_runner.py:927-1185`).
- **Pine reference:** alert dispatch is event text with no kernel-owned revision ledger (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/01_PINE/MTC_V2.pine:2008-2028`).
- **Disagreement:** a replay can duplicate orders, and changed data at the same timestamp can silently rewrite history. WP-P0-04 supplies the order/action/freshness vocabulary (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/contracts/mtc_contracts/orders.py:18-49`, `:52-127`; `MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/contracts/mtc_contracts/execution.py:16-53`).
- **Canonical semantics and reason:** bar identity is `(venue, symbol, timeframe, open_timestamp_utc)`. An exact duplicate payload is a **no-op**. The same identity with different OHLCV is `BAR_DRIFT` and blocks. Live out-of-order bars block; recovery sorts a bounded authoritative replay before acting. Intent ID is deterministic from deployment, worker, bar, action and position lifecycle; revisions are monotonic; a duplicate intent or revision is a no-op; a stale revision or cancel cannot overwrite newer state.
- **Chosen implementation:** **NEW + WP-P0-04 contracts**.
- **GF-26**
  1. **Type:** bar-driven plus static-validation.
  2. **Config:** `venue: "TESTVENUE"`, `symbol: "TESTUSDT"`, `timeframe: "1m"`, `mode: "LIVE"` for the ordering assertions and `mode: "RECOVERY"` for the sorted-replay assertion.
  3. **Frozen metadata:** `instrument_price_tick 0.01`, `instrument_qty_step 1.0`, `instrument_contract_multiplier 1`.
  4. **Bars:** baseline `B1 | 2026-01-01T00:00:00Z | 100,101,99,100,1`, `B2 | …:01 | 100,102,100,101,1`, `B3 | …:02 | 101,103,101,102,1`. Sequences fed: S1 `B1,B2,B3`; S2 `B1,B2,B2,B3` (exact duplicate); S3 `B1,B2,B2′,B3` where `B2′` is `B2` with close `104`; S4 `B1,B3,B2` in `LIVE` mode; S5 the same in `RECOVERY` mode.
  5. **Expected events:** S2 produces an event stream and a final state hash **identical** to S1, with no duplicated intent. S3 emits `BAR_DRIFT` at `B2′` and blocks. S4 emits `OUT_OF_ORDER_BAR` at `B2` and blocks. S5 sorts the bounded replay and then produces the S1 stream. Revision assertions: deliver revision 2 then revision 1 of one intent — state remains at revision 2; deliver cancel revision 3 twice — exactly one cancellation.
  6. **Expected fills/prices/quantities:** identical between S1 and S2; none after a block in S3 or S4.
  7. **Expected end state / reasons:** typed reasons `BAR_DRIFT`, `OUT_OF_ORDER_BAR`, `DUPLICATE_NOOP`, `STALE_REVISION_IGNORED`.
  8. **Discriminator (RED):** S2 and S3 against A and B, which simply process the extra bar and therefore produce a second entry in S2 and a rewritten history in S3 (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/runner.py:314-350`; `MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/engine/mtc_runner.py:927-1185`). A second RED replaces the deterministic intent ID with a random one and asserts the S1/S2 hash comparison fails.

### C27 — Restart and missed-decision recovery (ticket #45 policy; exact bound values decided here)

**Corrections across repair rounds 1 and 2.** Round 1 fixed this row's citation, which had pointed at a non-existent file, but it then declined to decide the bound values at all and recorded them as `[OPEN]`. Round 2 withdraws that deferral. The binding lane specification assigns the decision to this row in as many words: the ticket-#45 missed-decision policy is already decided **and** "Exact bound values are decided in this table's row" (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/11_TRIAGE/MASTER_WORK_PACKAGE_AND_PARALLEL_DELIVERY_PLAN_2026-08-22.md:377`). Deferring them was a package-contract violation; the numbers are decided below.

- **A behaviour:** an in-memory chronological runner with no durable replay and no action-freshness concept (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/runner.py:314-350`; `MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/types.py:71-169`).
- **B behaviour:** a batch runner, likewise not the restart authority (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/engine/mtc_runner.py:927-1185`).
- **Pine reference:** chart execution defines no Bridge restart replay semantics (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/01_PINE/MTC_V2.pine:2008-2028`).
- **Disagreement:** none of A, B or Pine decides when a decision discovered after downtime may still be acted on.
- **Canonical semantics and reason:** incorporate the already-decided policy — always replay missed bars to reconstruct state; act on a missed decision **only** within the freshness bound the existing freshness state machine defines; otherwise skip and log an explained divergence; the first action is at the next actionable bar close (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/11_TRIAGE/MASTER_WORK_PACKAGE_AND_PARALLEL_DELIVERY_PLAN_2026-08-22.md:377`; `MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/11_TRIAGE/WAYFINDER_DECISION_FOLD_2026-08-23.md:22`).
  The freshness state machine it refers to is §12.3 of the architecture brief, which defines `FRESH` as age below `interval + 15 s`, `AGING` as `interval + 15 s … + 45 s` with **warning only and no economic change**, and `STALE` as age above `interval + 45 s` with a hard block on new submissions while existing native stops remain live (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/11_TRIAGE/MASTER_ARCHITECTURE_AND_IMPLEMENTATION_BRIEF_2026-08-21.md:2317-2325`).
  **Age definition, decided.** `age = NTP-disciplined observation_utc − (venue_open_timestamp_utc + timeframe_seconds)`. Age is therefore measured from the **venue-authoritative bar close**, under C25's timestamp discipline, and from no host-local clock reading other than the NTP-disciplined observation instant. The brief writes the same quantity relative to the bar **open** and so carries `interval +` inside each threshold; the two forms are the same inequality and the decided thresholds below are stated once, in the bar-close form, to remove that ambiguity.
  **Bound values, decided.** This row closes the two numbers at exactly the values §12.3 documents, with the comparisons made explicit so no consumer has to infer an endpoint:

  | State | Decided comparison (bar-close age) | Equivalent bar-open form | Missed decision discovered in this state | Economic effect |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | `FRESH` | `age < 15 s` | `observation − open < timeframe + 15 s` | **acted on** | full trading under the normal risk policy |
  | `AGING` | `15 s ≤ age ≤ 45 s` | `timeframe + 15 s ≤ observation − open ≤ timeframe + 45 s` | **acted on** | **warning only — no economic change, and never a resize** |
  | `STALE` | `age > 45 s` | `observation − open > timeframe + 45 s` | **never acted on late** — skipped and logged as an explained divergence | hard block on new submissions; existing native stops remain live |

  The two boundaries are decided as **`15 s` and `45 s` exactly**, and **both endpoints belong to `AGING`**, so the three states partition the whole age axis with no gap and no overlap. `AGING` warns and never resizes, which is the brief's own rejected-sub-clause rule carried forward rather than re-argued (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/11_TRIAGE/MASTER_ARCHITECTURE_AND_IMPLEMENTATION_BRIEF_2026-08-21.md:2329`). Both boundaries are **frozen-package constants**: a package that omits either does not build, and a consumer handed an unset bound fails closed rather than reading the absence as permission (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/contracts/README.md:36-38`).
  **Why the `[OPEN]` marker does not block this.** The brief's carrier-assignment paragraph marks the numerical boundaries `[OPEN]` (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/11_TRIAGE/MASTER_ARCHITECTURE_AND_IMPLEMENTATION_BRIEF_2026-08-21.md:2332`) because that paragraph assigns owners, not values. The delivery plan names WP-P0-09 as the owner of the exact values, so closing them here is this lane's mandate and not a re-opening of ticket #45: the policy shape is ticket #45's and is unchanged, and only the two numbers move from documented-and-open to decided. The previous revision's `venue open + timeframe + 45 s` phrasing is withdrawn as imprecise — the deadline runs from bar close, and `+ 45 s` is the **inclusive end of `AGING`**, not the first stale instant.
- **Chosen implementation:** **ticket #45 decision for the policy shape; this row decides the exact bound values** — `15 s` and `45 s`, both endpoints inclusive to `AGING` — as the delivery plan requires.
- **GF-27**
  1. **Type:** bar-driven with injected observation timestamps.
  2. **Config:** `timeframe: "60s"`; `freshness_bound: {fresh_below_s: 15, aging_upper_s: 45, aging_endpoints_inclusive: true}` — these are the values **decided above**, restated here as the frozen-package constants the fixture asserts, not as a fixture-local choice. Run R5 supplies `freshness_bound: null` to exercise the defence **behind** the build-time guard: even if a package were somehow constructed without the constants, no consumer may read that absence as permission.
  3. **Frozen metadata:** venue `TESTVENUE`, symbol `TESTUSDT`; NTP offset pinned to zero.
  4. **Bars:** one 60-second bar opening `2026-01-01T12:00:00Z`, therefore closing `12:01:00Z`, `O=100,H=101,L=99,C=101,V=1`, carrying a valid long decision. Restart observation timestamps, one per run: R1 `12:01:14.999Z`, R2 `12:01:15.000Z`, R3 `12:01:45.000Z`, R4 `12:01:45.001Z`.
  5. **Expected events:** all runs replay the bar and reconstruct **identical** internal state. R1 emits one order tagged `FRESH`; R2 and R3 emit one order tagged `AGING`; R4 emits **no** order and logs `MISSED_DECISION_STALE`. R5 emits no order and logs `FRESHNESS_BOUND_UNSET_FAIL_CLOSED`.
  6. **Expected fills/prices/quantities:** at most one order per run; the reconstructed indicator state is byte-identical across all five runs.
  7. **Expected end state / reasons:** typed reasons `FRESH`, `AGING`, `MISSED_DECISION_STALE`, `FRESHNESS_BOUND_UNSET_FAIL_CLOSED`. The next live decision may act only at its own bar close.
  8. **Discriminator (RED):** three REDs. (i) R4 against any implementation that acts on a replayed decision without an age test — both A and B do, because neither has one (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/runner.py:314-350`; `MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/engine/mtc_runner.py:927-1185`). (ii) R5 against any implementation that treats an unset bound as permissive, which the contract rule forbids (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/contracts/README.md:36-38`). (iii) **The decided numbers themselves are pinned:** mutate `aging_upper_s` from `45` to `30` and R3 must flip from `AGING`-with-order to `MISSED_DECISION_STALE`-with-no-order; mutate `fresh_below_s` from `15` to `20` and R2 must flip from `AGING` to `FRESH`; set `aging_endpoints_inclusive: false` and R2 and R3 must both change state. Each mutation must go RED, which is what makes `15 s` / `45 s` and the inclusive endpoints a decided rule rather than a comment.

### C28 — WunderTrading route-code inputs (all five route `wt_*` keys)

**Citation corrected in repair round 1; the negative-search evidence was expanded in repair round 6.** The previous revision supported the "no consumers" claim with an architecture-audit reference to a file that does not exist. Round 1 replaced it with a pointer to a recorded negative search, but the pointer was written as a bare document name and was the claim's only support. The claim is now supported on this line by the primary sources and by the reproducible search itself: `rg -n "wt_" MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2` matches **one file only** — `MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/config.py:226-238`, where the five route names are declared at `:226-230` and where validation begins only at `:569`, skipping them entirely (`:569-580`) — so no A module outside the declaration reads them. The sweep row is cross-referenced at `MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/11_TRIAGE/WP_P0_09_CAPABILITY_TABLE_2026-08-25/COVERAGE_SWEEP.md` §8.

- **A behaviour:** declares `wt_enter_long_code`, `wt_exit_long_code`, `wt_enter_short_code`, `wt_exit_short_code` and `wt_exit_all_code` as empty strings (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/config.py:226-230`). A precise additional fact: these five are the **only** `wt_*` keys that `validate_config` never type-checks — validation begins at `wt_order_type` (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/config.py:569-580`). They have no runtime consumer anywhere in A.
- **B behaviour:** `rg -n "wt_" MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src` returns **no match in any file**, so B has no WunderTrading capability of any kind and declares no route surface in `MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/config/defaults.py:406-451`. Cross-reference: `MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/11_TRIAGE/WP_P0_09_CAPABILITY_TABLE_2026-08-25/COVERAGE_SWEEP.md` §8.
- **Pine reference:** declares the same five as user inputs (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/01_PINE/MTC_V2.pine:176-180`), gates the whole dispatch block on "any code non-empty" (`:2010`), and serialises them into live `alert()` calls (`:2012-2028`).
- **Disagreement:** Pine can arm an unaudited live route by typing a code into an input box, while A stores dead configuration it does not even validate, and B has no such capability at all.
- **Canonical semantics and reason:** these are Bridge/execution routing credentials, not strategy economics. Retire all five from the kernel and from Pine after reproduction; the Bridge resolves a route from an authenticated deployment identity. An empty code must never mean implicit authorisation. This matches the already-approved WP-P0-23 removal scope, which names both the two `alert()` lines and the thirteen Python keys (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/11_TRIAGE/MASTER_WORK_PACKAGE_AND_PARALLEL_DELIVERY_PLAN_2026-08-22.md:539-543`).
- **Chosen implementation:** **RETIRE** all five; no canonical kernel replacement.
- **GF-28**
  1. **Type:** static-validation plus one bar-driven legacy run.
  2. **Config:** static block asserts the exact key set `{wt_enter_long_code, wt_exit_long_code, wt_enter_short_code, wt_exit_short_code, wt_exit_all_code}` — five names, no more and no fewer. Legacy run L1 sets all five to `""`; L2 sets only `wt_enter_long_code: "ENTER_L_TEST"`; L3 sets only `wt_exit_all_code: "EXIT_ALL_TEST"`.
  3. **Frozen metadata:** `instrument_qty_step 1.0`, `instrument_contract_multiplier 1`.
  4. **Bars:** `0 | 2026-01-01T00:00:00Z | 100,100,100,100,1` raw `(1,0)` opening a long; `1 | …:01 | 100,100,100,100,1` raw `(0,1)` closing it.
  5. **Expected events:** L1 — **zero** alert payloads over both bars. L2 — exactly one payload, on bar 0, whose `code` field is `"ENTER_L_TEST"`; no exit payload, because `wt_exit_long_code` and `wt_exit_all_code` are both empty. L3 — exactly one payload, on bar 1, whose `code` is `"EXIT_ALL_TEST"` and whose `reduce_only` is the hard-coded `true`.
  6. **Expected fills/prices/quantities:** identical entry and exit fills in L1, L2 and L3 — the route codes must not change any economic outcome, and the fixture asserts a byte-identical economic event stream and state hash across the three runs.
  7. **Expected end state / reasons:** corrected-vNext block — a kernel configuration containing any of the five names is **rejected** with `UNKNOWN_CONFIG_KEY`, and the route is resolved solely from an authenticated Bridge deployment fixture.
  8. **Discriminator (RED):** L2 against current Pine, where a single non-empty code flips `l25_any_code_set` and arms live dispatch from a text input (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/01_PINE/MTC_V2.pine:2010-2020`). The corrected-vNext block must fail RED against current A, which accepts all five keys silently and does not even type-check them (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/config.py:226-230`, `:569-580`).

### C29 — WunderTrading order-payload inputs (all four payload `wt_*` keys)

- **A behaviour:** declares `wt_order_type` (`"market"`), `wt_amount_type` (`"quote"`), `wt_amount` (`100.0`) and `wt_leverage` (`1`) (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/config.py:231-234`) and validates their types and enumerations (`:569-576`), but no A runtime path reads them: `rg -n "wt_" MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2` matches only `MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/config.py`, so the declaration and its validator are the only readers. Cross-reference: `MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/11_TRIAGE/WP_P0_09_CAPABILITY_TABLE_2026-08-25/COVERAGE_SWEEP.md` §8.
- **B behaviour:** `rg -n "wt_" MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src` returns no match; B declares no order-payload surface (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/11_TRIAGE/WP_P0_09_CAPABILITY_TABLE_2026-08-25/COVERAGE_SWEEP.md` §8).
- **Pine reference:** declares the same four (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/01_PINE/MTC_V2.pine:181-184`) and serialises `order_type`, `amount`, `amount_type` and `leverage` directly into the entry alert payload (`:2020`).
- **Disagreement:** the Pine payload's size and leverage are **independent of the strategy's own calculated position**, so the alert can order a different economic quantity than the strategy modelled; A merely accepts the keys.
- **Canonical semantics and reason:** retire all four. The kernel emits a typed `OrderIntent` plus a `SizingRequest`; the Risk Allocator and Guardian produce the authorised quantity; the Bridge translates that into a venue order type and quantity. Leverage is a risk/allocation input, never free alert text (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/contracts/mtc_contracts/sizing.py:39-121`; `MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/contracts/mtc_contracts/orders.py:27-49`, `:52-101`).
- **Chosen implementation:** **RETIRE** all four in favour of the WP-P0-04 contracts.
- **GF-29**
  1. **Type:** static-validation. **The legacy payload is asserted, not recorded:** Pine line 2020 is a literal string concatenation of constants and inputs, with no strategy state in it, so the exact bytes follow from the source line and the input defaults by inspection.
  2. **Config:** static block asserts the exact key set `{wt_order_type, wt_amount_type, wt_amount, wt_leverage}` — four names. The legacy arm uses the declared Pine input defaults `wt_order_type "market"`, `wt_amount_type "quote"`, `wt_amount 100.0`, `wt_leverage 1` (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/01_PINE/MTC_V2.pine:181-184`), the declared flag defaults `wt_use_tp false`, `wt_use_sl false`, `wt_reduce_only true`, `wt_place_cond_orders false` (`:185-188`), and `wt_enter_long_code: "ENTER_L_TEST"` so the entry dispatch is armed at all (`:2010`, `:2016`).
  3. **Frozen metadata:** `instrument_qty_step 1.0`, `instrument_contract_multiplier 1`.
  4. **Bars:** the GF-28 two-bar sequence.
  5. **Expected events:** exactly one entry payload, on bar 0, whose bytes are asserted in full:

     ```
     {"code":"ENTER_L_TEST","order_type":"market","amount":100,"amount_type":"quote","leverage":1,"reduce_only":true}
     ```

     Every segment of that string is read off the concatenation at `MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/01_PINE/MTC_V2.pine:2020`: the three optional segments `l25_tp_str`, `l25_sl_str` and `l25_cond_str` are the empty string under the three `false` flag defaults (`:2017-2019`), and `reduce_only` renders `true` from `wt_reduce_only` (`:2020`). The one element whose rendering is a runtime property rather than a literal is `str.tostring(wt_amount)`; the fixture therefore asserts it **twice** — as the character sequence `100` for the `input.float(100.0)` default, and as a second arm with `wt_amount: 250.5` asserting the character sequence `250.5` — so a formatting change is a visible fixture failure rather than an unstated assumption. Corrected-vNext block: changing any of the four retired fields produces a **byte-identical** canonical `OrderIntent`, while changing the Guardian-authorised quantity changes it.
  6. **Expected fills/prices/quantities:** the canonical intent's quantity comes only from the authorised allocation; an unsupported limit semantic yields `NON_EXPRESSIBLE`, never a silent market fallback.
  7. **Expected end state / reasons:** typed reason `NON_EXPRESSIBLE`; corrected-vNext rejects all four names with `UNKNOWN_CONFIG_KEY`.
  8. **Discriminator (RED):** set `wt_amount: 100` while the strategy's own authorised quantity is a different number, and assert that the legacy Pine payload still orders `100` quote units (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/01_PINE/MTC_V2.pine:2020`) — a divergence between modelled and ordered size that the corrected path cannot express.

### C30 — WunderTrading protective flags (all four protective `wt_*` keys)

**A content claim in the previous revision was imprecise and is corrected here.** Two of these four keys are not consumerless: they participate in A's configuration validation.

- **A behaviour:** declares `wt_use_tp` (`False`), `wt_use_sl` (`False`), `wt_reduce_only` (`True`) and `wt_place_cond_orders` (`False`) (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/config.py:235-238`). `wt_use_tp` and `wt_use_sl` have a **real cross-validation consumer**: a configuration with `wt_use_tp=True` and `use_tp=False`, or `wt_use_sl=True` and `use_sl=False`, is rejected outright (`:581-584`). `wt_reduce_only` and `wt_place_cond_orders` are type-checked only (`:579-580`) and have no runtime consumer.
- **B behaviour:** `rg -n "wt_" MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src` returns no match; B declares no protective-flag surface (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/11_TRIAGE/WP_P0_09_CAPABILITY_TABLE_2026-08-25/COVERAGE_SWEEP.md` §8).
- **Pine reference:** declares all four (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/01_PINE/MTC_V2.pine:185-188`) and writes them into the live entry payload; note that `reduce_only` is serialised **on the entry alert** from the user flag, while the exit alert hard-codes `"reduce_only":true` (`:2017-2020`, `:2027-2028`).
- **Disagreement:** payload flags can contradict both the kernel's own stop/target state and the venue's capabilities, and Pine's entry-side `reduce_only` is a flag that cannot be correct for an opening order.
- **Canonical semantics and reason:** retire all four from strategy configuration. Protective intent derives from the canonical position lifecycle; reduce-only and conditional-order realisation are Bridge/venue policy validated against venue capabilities. No boolean may silently disable an economically required stop, and no opening order may carry reduce-only.
- **Chosen implementation:** **RETIRE** all four; A's `wt_use_tp` / `wt_use_sl` cross-validation is retired with them, because C11/C12 already make a required-but-unavailable stop or target a typed block.
- **GF-30**
  1. **Type:** static-validation plus one bar-driven legacy run.
  2. **Config:** static block asserts the exact key set `{wt_use_tp, wt_use_sl, wt_reduce_only, wt_place_cond_orders}` — four names. Legacy runs enumerate the four all-false and all-true permutations the serialiser actually branches on. A fifth static case sets `wt_use_tp: true` with `use_tp: false`.
  3. **Frozen metadata:** `instrument_price_tick 0.01`, `instrument_qty_step 1.0`, `instrument_contract_multiplier 1`.
  4. **Bars:** the GF-28 two-bar sequence, with the entry carrying a required stop `95.00` and target `105.00`.
  5. **Expected events:** all-false — the entry payload contains neither `"tp"` nor `"sl"` nor `"place_conditional_orders"`, and `"reduce_only":false` **on an opening order**. All-true — all three appear and `"reduce_only":true` on the opening order. Fifth case — A raises `wt_use_tp requires use_tp=True` (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/config.py:581-582`).
  6. **Expected fills/prices/quantities:** identical economic fills across every permutation; the flags change only the payload text.
  7. **Expected end state / reasons:** corrected-vNext — a long entry with a required stop and target produces Bridge translations that are reduce-only for **exits only** and that create supported conditional orders independently of any retired boolean; an unsupported protection blocks deployment with `PROTECTION_NOT_SUPPORTED`.
  8. **Discriminator (RED):** the all-false legacy run against the corrected rule — legacy emits an entry payload with **no stop** while the kernel holds a required stop of `95.00`, which the corrected rule forbids (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/01_PINE/MTC_V2.pine:2017-2020`). A second RED asserts that `"reduce_only":true` on an opening order is rejected by the corrected Bridge translation.

### C31 — `tw_audit_semantics_mode`

**Citation corrected in repair round 1.** The previous revision cited a non-existent architecture-audit file for this key's behaviour; the branches are now cited to the code that implements them.

- **A behaviour:** a required string validated to `{"off", "research"}` (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/config.py:58`, `:282`, `:470-471`). It is the **master gate for every other `tw_*` branch**: the sizer's alternate quantity precision (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/position_sizer.py:22`, `:61-62`), the BE and trailing semantics branches (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/exits.py:294-298`, `:302`, `:334`), the margin-call branch (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/runner.py:563-564`) and every reversal-re-entry branch (`:1293-1295`, `:1310-1313`, `:1322-1325`, `:1334-1337`) all require `mode == "research"` before any other `tw_*` value has effect.
- **B behaviour:** `rg -n "tw_" MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src` returns **no match in any file**; B has no TradingView compatibility switch of any kind (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/11_TRIAGE/WP_P0_09_CAPABILITY_TABLE_2026-08-25/COVERAGE_SWEEP.md` §8).
- **Pine reference:** a constant `"off"` with no consumer (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/01_PINE/MTC_V2.pine:95`).
- **Disagreement:** a single broad "audit" switch silently changes quantity precision and five other economic behaviours in A, is inert in Pine, and is absent from B.
- **Canonical semantics and reason:** reproduce both A branches, then retire the master switch. Precision is explicit instrument/profile metadata (C09), never an "audit" mode. **Consequence recorded for the other `tw_*` rows:** because this key gates them all, every legacy fixture for C32–C34, C36 and C37 must set `tw_audit_semantics_mode: "research"` or it will observe no branch at all.
- **Chosen implementation:** **RETIRE** after legacy reproduction.
- **GF-31**
  1. **Type:** static-validation.
  2. **Config:** two legacy runs, `tw_audit_semantics_mode: "off"` and `"research"`, over the same sizing call; one corrected-vNext case with the key absent.
  3. **Frozen metadata:** `instrument_qty_step 0.01`, `instrument_min_qty 0`, `instrument_min_notional 0`, `instrument_contract_multiplier 1`.
  4. **Bars:** none; the input is a single raw quantity of `1.234567`.
  5. **Expected events:** one rounding result per run.
  6. **Expected fills/prices/quantities:** `"off"` floors to the instrument step and yields exactly `1.23`; `"research"` replaces that with a fixed six-decimal floor and yields exactly `1.234567` — both derived directly from the cited arithmetic (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/position_sizer.py:60-62`), so no recorded snapshot is needed. Corrected-vNext yields `1.23` from instrument metadata alone.
  7. **Expected end state / reasons:** corrected-vNext rejects the key with `UNKNOWN_CONFIG_KEY`.
  8. **Discriminator (RED):** the corrected-vNext case against A, which accepts the key and changes the executable quantity by `0.004567` on this single input (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/position_sizer.py:61-62`). A second RED asserts that with `tw_audit_semantics_mode: "off"` **no** other `tw_*` value can change any result, proving the gate.

### C32 — `tw_reversal_reentry_mode`

- **A behaviour:** a required string validated to exactly five values — `local`, `delay_after_protective_exit`, `carry_to_next_bar_after_protective_exit`, `next_bar_open_after_protective_exit_signal`, `next_bar_close_after_protective_exit_signal` (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/config.py:59`, `:283`, `:472-483`). The four non-`local` values feed three distinct deferral predicates, each of which additionally requires `tw_audit_semantics_mode == "research"` **and** `tw_reversal_reentry_delay_bars > 0` (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/runner.py:1293-1308`, `:1310-1320`, `:1322-1332`), and a queued side is executed on a later bar (`:1334-1348`, `:1396-1407`). The last protective exit bar is recorded for every non-opposite exit reason (`:1289-1291`).
- **B behaviour:** no `tw_*` key; its separate same-bar re-entry controls are C21's subject (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/config/defaults.py:299-314`).
- **Pine reference:** a constant `"local"` with no consumer (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/01_PINE/MTC_V2.pine:96`).
- **Disagreement:** A offers five different re-entry economics after a protective exit, with no Pine branch authority behind any of them.
- **Canonical semantics and reason:** retire the compatibility mode. C05 and C21 are the sole reversal and re-entry policy: a protective exit never re-enters on the same bar, and no stale decision is carried to a later bar.
- **Chosen implementation:** **RETIRE**, after reproducing every accepted legacy enum value.
- **GF-32**
  1. **Type:** bar-driven and **fully asserted for all five legacy runs and for the corrected-vNext run.** The round-3 revision gave the five legacy arms the record-it-later type; that type is withdrawn (§1.4). Two defects were found in doing so, and both are repaired below: the round-3 bar 1 **could not stop at all** under the profile the fixture itself declared, so none of the five branches was reachable; and `tw_reversal_reentry_delay_bars: 1` collapsed three of the five enum outcomes onto one bar, so even a correct recording would not have discriminated them.
  2. **Config:** common to all five legacy runs — **`tw_audit_semantics_mode: "research"`** (mandatory; without it none of these values does anything, `MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/runner.py:1294-1295`, `:1311-1312`, `:1323-1324`), **`tw_reversal_reentry_delay_bars: 2`**, `use_sl: true`, `use_sl_percent: true`, `sl_percent: 5.0`, `tp_mode: "None"`, `exit_on_opposite_signal: true`, `allow_flip: true`, `execution_profile_id: "close_only_deterministic_v2"`, `initial_capital: 1000`, `risk_per_long_pct: 0.5`, `risk_per_short_pct: 0.5`, `max_leverage_cap: 1.0`, `cooldown_bars: 0`, `max_entries: 1`, `regime_lock: false`, all gates disabled. One run per enum value: `local`, `delay_after_protective_exit`, `carry_to_next_bar_after_protective_exit`, `next_bar_open_after_protective_exit_signal`, `next_bar_close_after_protective_exit_signal`. The corrected-vNext run drops every `tw_*` key and uses GF-20's canonical `execution_profile: "STANDING_TOUCH"`.

     **Why the sizing keys are stated this way.** A has no `FIXED_QTY` sizer: `PositionSizer.calc_qty` has exactly two quantity paths — risk-at-stop when a stop exists, and a percent-of-equity fallback when it does not — followed by a leverage cap (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/position_sizer.py:43-55`). The round-3 field 3 said "entry quantity `1` via `FIXED_QTY`", which A cannot express; A's `fixed_qty` key is in fact **rejected unless it equals `1.0`** and controls nothing (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/config.py:457-462`). The keys above produce the quantity `1` through the path A actually has, and the quantity is asserted in field 6.
  3. **Frozen metadata:** `instrument_price_tick 0.01`, **`instrument_qty_step 0.000001`**, `instrument_min_qty 0`, `instrument_min_notional 0`, `instrument_contract_multiplier 1`. The step is `0.000001` deliberately: at that step A's `research`-mode six-decimal quantity floor and its ordinary `floor_qty` step floor agree exactly (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/position_sizer.py:60-62`), so this fixture observes the re-entry timing alone and never confounds it with GF-31's rounding branch.
  4. **Bars:** six bars, volume `1` throughout.

     | Index | Timestamp | `O,H,L,C` | Injected raw | Why this bar exists |
     |---|---|---|---|---|
     | 0 | `2026-01-01T00:00:00Z` | `100,100,100,100` | `(1,0)` | entry |
     | 1 | `2026-01-01T00:01:00Z` | `99,99,94,94` | `(1,0)` | protective exit; also carries a fresh same-direction raw so the carry branch has a side to queue |
     | 2 | `2026-01-01T00:02:00Z` | `94,100,94,100` | `(1,0)` | earliest bar with its own signal |
     | 3 | `2026-01-01T00:03:00Z` | `99,100,99,100` | `(0,0)` | **no** signal; open `99` differs from close `100` |
     | 4 | `2026-01-01T00:04:00Z` | `100,100,99,100` | `(0,0)` | **no** signal |
     | 5 | `2026-01-01T00:05:00Z` | `100,100,99,100` | `(1,0)` | fresh signal after every deferral window has expired |

     **Bar 1 is the round-4 repair.** Its previous close was `96`, and under `close_only_deterministic_v2` a long stop fills only when `bar.close <= stop_price` (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/exits.py:434-443`, dispatched at `:359-360`, `:560-561`); `96 > 95.00`, so that bar produced **no** protective exit, the runner never recorded a protective-exit bar index (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/runner.py:1289-1291`), and every one of the five branches short-circuited on its own `is None` guard (`:1303-1304`, `:1317-1318`, `:1329-1330`) to a no-op. The close is now `94`, which is at or below the stop, so the branch is reachable and the exit fills at `94.00`.
  5. **Expected events:** all five legacy runs share bar 0 `ENTER_LONG` and bar 1 `EXIT_LONG(reason=sl_percent_hit)`. They then diverge, and **every divergence is asserted, with the predicate that produces it**. Let `n = bar_index − 1` be the bars since the protective exit.

     | Run | Re-entry bar | Fill | Deciding predicate |
     |---|---|---|---|
     | `local` | bar 2 | close `100.00` | no deferral predicate applies; bar 1 is blocked by its own exit and bar 2 is the first bar with a live signal |
     | `carry_to_next_bar_after_protective_exit` | bar 3 | close `100.00` | side queued at bar 1 (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/runner.py:956-965`); deferral is `n < 2` (`:1306-1307`), true at bar 2 and false at bar 3; the queued side is re-offered on a bar with **no raw signal at all** (`:1393-1409`) |
     | `next_bar_open_after_protective_exit_signal` | bar 3 | **open `99.00`** | queue predicate `0 < n <= 2` (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/runner.py:1320`) fires on bar 2's live decision and suppresses it (`:968-975`); the queued entry executes at the **top** of bar 3 against `bar.open` (`:350`, `:1334-1391`) |
     | `next_bar_close_after_protective_exit_signal` | bar 4 | close `100.00` | queue predicate `0 < n <= 2` (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/runner.py:1332`) fires at bar 2 and **again at bar 3** (`n = 2`), re-queuing and suppressing each time (`:976-983`); at bar 4 `n = 3` fails the predicate and the pending decision executes at that bar's close |
     | `delay_after_protective_exit` | bar 5 | close `100.00` | deferral is `n <= 2` (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/runner.py:1308`), true at bars 2 and 3, false from bar 4; this mode queues **nothing** (`:1393-1399` lists only the two carry modes), so it needs a bar that is both past the window and carries its own raw signal — bar 4 has none, bar 5 does |

     Five runs, **four distinct re-entry bars and two distinct fill prices**, all forced by the two comparison operators and the queue mechanism rather than by a recorded number. The reason string on every legacy exit is A's own constant `sl_percent_hit` (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/exits.py:22`, selected at `:570-571`), and the fixture asserts that literal rather than a canonical alias. The corrected-vNext run asserts, in full: bar 0 `ENTER_LONG` at `100.00`; bar 1 `EXIT_LONG` at `95.00` with the canonical typed reason `SL_PERCENT` **and no entry on that bar**, because C21 blocks every new entry on the bar of a protective exit; **bar 2 `ENTER_LONG` at close `100.00`**; no decision and no event on bars 3 and 4; and bar 5 `BLOCK(reason=MAX_ENTRIES)`. **The bar-2 re-entry is a round-5 repair, and the oracle it replaces was wrong on a predicate, not on a number.** The previous revision asserted no entry on bars 1 through 5 and read C21 and C05 as forbidding both live raw signals. Neither predicate reaches bar 2: C21 blocks new entries on **the bar of** the protective exit and says nothing about later bars (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/position_manager.py:119-121`, `:137-142`), and C05 forbids a **carried** decision — one taken on an earlier bar's information — not a fresh decision taken on a later bar's own. Bar 2 carries its own fresh raw `(1,0)`, this run sets `cooldown_bars: 0` and declares no post-exit cooldown key at all, and every gate is disabled, so no decided rule refuses it and the decided rule **must** enter there. Bars 3 and 4 carry no raw signal, so there is no decision to act on and no block reason is emitted. Bar 5's raw `(1,0)` is fresh and otherwise admissible, and is refused only because `max_entries: 1` and the bar-2 position is still open — the fixture asserts that refusal as a typed `MAX_ENTRIES` block, not as an absence. The bar-2 entry's stop of `95.00` is a standing order active from the next tradable instant after entry (C11), so bar 2's own low of `94` is not eligible to fill it, and no later bar reaches it — bars 3, 4 and 5 all bottom at `99`.
  6. **Expected fills/prices/quantities:** the bar-0 entry is `1.000000 @ 100.00` — `1000 × (0.5 / 100) = 5.00` of risk over a per-unit risk of `|100 − 95.00| = 5.00` gives `1.0`, under a leverage cap of `(1000 × 1.0) / 100 = 10.0` that does not bind (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/position_sizer.py:43-55`). The legacy stop fills `1.000000 @ 94.00` for a realised `−6.00`, leaving a sizing equity snapshot of `994.00` (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/runner.py:1504-1505`). Each legacy re-entry quantity is therefore asserted exactly: the four runs that re-enter at a close of `100.00` size `994 × 0.005 / 5.00 = 0.994000`; the `next_bar_open` run enters at `99.00` against a stop of `94.05`, so `994 × 0.005 / 4.95 = 1.004040` after the six-decimal floor. The corrected-vNext run has exactly **three** fills in the whole sequence — the bar-0 entry `1.000000 @ 100.00`; the bar-1 stop `1.000000 @ 95.00`, which is the **touch** price rather than the close, because the corrected profile is `STANDING_TOUCH` (GF-20 R1); and the **bar-2 re-entry `0.995000 @ 100.00`**. That third quantity is derived by the same arithmetic the legacy re-entries use, over the equity the touch fill leaves: realised `−5.00` gives a sizing snapshot of `995.00` (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/runner.py:1504-1505`), the bar-2 close of `100.00` under `sl_percent: 5.0` gives a stop of `95.00` and a per-unit risk of `5.00`, and `995 × 0.005 / 5.00 = 0.995000` exactly, with no six-decimal-floor remainder to discard. The leverage cap does not bind on either basis — `(1000 × 1.0) / 100 = 10.0` and `(995 × 1.0) / 100 = 9.95`, both far above the required `0.995000`. Bar 5 adds no fill: `max_entries: 1`.
  7. **Expected end state / reasons:** the legacy runs are labelled `legacy, non-canonical` and may not be aggregated with the corrected run. Legacy end states, asserted: `local` long `0.994000` open from bar 2; `carry` long `0.994000` open from bar 3; `next_bar_open` long `1.004040` open from bar 3; `next_bar_close` long `0.994000` open from bar 4; `delay` long `0.994000` open from bar 5. Corrected-vNext ends **long `0.995000`, open from bar 2**, with realised `−5.00` from the bar-1 stop and typed reasons `SL_PERCENT` at bar 1 and `MAX_ENTRIES` at bar 5. **It does not end flat, and the fixture asserts an open position rather than an absence.** This end state and GF-33's corrected end state — long `0.995000` from **bar 5** — are not in conflict and differ for exactly one declared reason: GF-33's corrected arm sets `post_exit_cooldown_bars: 2` (C06), which blocks its bars 2 and 3, while this run declares no post-exit cooldown and sets `cooldown_bars: 0`, so bar 2 is admissible here. The two arms share the same bars, the same `−5.00` realised and the same `0.995000` size; only the cooldown key, and therefore the entry bar, differs. Opposite-signal reversal is asserted separately in GF-05 and must be **unaffected** by this key — the fixture re-runs GF-05 under all five enum values and asserts an identical event stream.
  8. **Discriminator (RED):** the corrected-vNext run — re-entry at **bar 2, close `100.00`, quantity `0.995000`** — against each of the five legacy runs. **These are five REDs on three named axes, and the axis is stated per run rather than aggregated. This fixture does not claim five distinct RED event timings, and the previous revision's claim that it discriminated against "a corrected run that takes no re-entry at all" is withdrawn with the flat oracle that produced it.** `local` re-enters on **the same bar at the same price** and differs on the **quantity axis alone** — `0.994000` against `0.995000` — the entire difference being the protective fill that precedes it: close-only `94.00` against standing-touch `95.00` (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/exits.py:434-443`), realised `−6.00` against `−5.00`, sizing snapshot `994.00` against `995.00`. `carry_to_next_bar_after_protective_exit` differs on **bar and quantity** — bar 3 at `100.00` for `0.994000` — and enters on a bar with no raw signal of its own, on a side decided two bars earlier (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/runner.py:956-965`, `:1393-1409`). `next_bar_open_after_protective_exit_signal` differs on **bar, price and quantity** — bar 3 at the open `99.00` for `1.004040` (`:1320`, `:1334-1391`). `next_bar_close_after_protective_exit_signal` differs on **bar and quantity** — bar 4 at `100.00` for `0.994000` (`:1332`). `delay_after_protective_exit` differs on **bar and quantity** — bar 5 at `100.00` for `0.994000` (`:1308`). Counted across the five: the **bar** axis yields three distinct later bars (3, 3, 4, 5, with two runs sharing bar 3), the **price** axis exactly one departure from `100.00`, and the **quantity** axis all five. **The `local` RED is the one no event-level comparison can see** — same bar, same price, same event type — and only the asserted quantity exposes it, which is why field 6 asserts quantities and why a comparison of event streams alone would admit this legacy mode as canonical. It is the same fill-semantics residue GF-33 RED (iii) isolates, arriving one bar earlier because this run declares no post-exit cooldown. A control RED sets each enum with `tw_audit_semantics_mode: "off"` and asserts all five collapse to the `local` result, proving the master gate (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/runner.py:1294-1295`).

### C33 — `tw_reversal_reentry_delay_bars`

- **A behaviour:** a required non-negative integer (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/config.py:60`, `:284`). Every predicate that reads it returns `False` immediately when it is `<= 0`, so a delay of `0` disables the legacy path entirely regardless of the mode (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/runner.py:1301-1302`, `:1315-1316`, `:1327-1328`). The comparison is `bars_since_exit < delay` for the carry mode and `<= delay` for the delay mode (`:1305-1308`).
- **B behaviour:** no `tw_*` key; unrelated re-entry controls only (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/config/defaults.py:299-314`).
- **Pine reference:** a constant `0` with no consumer (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/01_PINE/MTC_V2.pine:97`).
- **Disagreement:** only A assigns economic meaning to it, only in combination with a legacy mode and the research gate, and the two comparison operators differ between modes.
- **Canonical semantics and reason:** retire it. A delayed stale decision is forbidden by C05 and C27; post-exit cooldown is the explicit C06 concept and always requires a **fresh** decision on the acting bar.
- **Chosen implementation:** **RETIRE**, after branch reproduction.
- **GF-33**
  1. **Type:** bar-driven and **fully asserted for every arm, legacy and corrected alike.** The round-3 revision left the `delay = 2` arm's re-entry bar and fill price to be recorded later; that deferral is withdrawn and the values are derived below from the two comparison operators.
  2. **Config:** three legacy arms, all with `tw_audit_semantics_mode: "research"` and every other key exactly as in GF-32 — **D0** `{tw_reversal_reentry_mode: "delay_after_protective_exit", tw_reversal_reentry_delay_bars: 0}`; **D2** the same mode with `tw_reversal_reentry_delay_bars: 2`; **C2** `{tw_reversal_reentry_mode: "carry_to_next_bar_after_protective_exit", tw_reversal_reentry_delay_bars: 2}`, which differs from D2 in the **mode only** and therefore isolates the operator. Corrected-vNext uses `post_exit_cooldown_bars: 2`, no `tw_*` key, and GF-20's canonical `execution_profile: "STANDING_TOUCH"`.
  3. **Frozen metadata:** identical to GF-32, including `instrument_qty_step 0.000001`.
  4. **Bars:** the GF-32 six-bar sequence, unchanged and reused verbatim, so the two fixtures' arithmetic is shared and cross-checkable.
  5. **Expected events, all asserted.** Let `n = bar_index − 1` be the bars since the bar-1 protective exit.
     - **D0** is **identical to GF-32's `local` run** in every field — the predicate returns `False` before reading anything else when the delay is not positive (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/runner.py:1301-1302`), so the key disables its own mode. Re-entry at bar 2, close `100.00`.
     - **D2** defers while `n <= 2` (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/runner.py:1308`), i.e. on bars 2 and 3, and queues nothing — the pending-decision path admits only the two carry modes (`:1393-1399`). Bar 4 is past the window but carries no raw signal, so nothing is decided there; bar 5 carries a fresh raw signal. Re-entry at **bar 5**, close `100.00`.
     - **C2** defers while `n < 2` (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/runner.py:1306-1307`), i.e. on bar 2 only, and **does** queue the side observed at bar 1 (`:956-965`). Re-entry at **bar 3**, close `100.00`, on a bar with no raw signal of its own.
     - **The `<` / `<=` asymmetry is the whole finding of this row and is asserted as such.** D2 and C2 hold the *same* `tw_reversal_reentry_delay_bars: 2`; the deferral windows they produce are `{1, 2}` and `{1}` respectively, so their first admissible bars are 4 and 3. One integer key means two different windows depending on a second key, which is why the value cannot be given a canonical meaning and the key is retired rather than renamed.
     - **Corrected-vNext:** bar 1 `EXIT_LONG(reason=SL_PERCENT)` at `95.00`; bars 2 and 3 `BLOCK(reason=POST_EXIT_COOLDOWN)`; bar 4 is past the cooldown but carries **no decision**, so no entry and no block reason is emitted; bar 5 carries a fresh raw signal and produces `ENTER_LONG` at close `100.00`.
  6. **Expected fills/prices/quantities:** the bar-0 entry is `1.000000 @ 100.00` in every arm. The three legacy arms exit at the close-only price `94.00` for a realised `−6.00` and a sizing snapshot of `994.00`, so each legacy re-entry is `994 × 0.005 / 5.00 = 0.994000`. The corrected run exits at the standing-touch price `95.00` for a realised `−5.00` and a snapshot of `995.00`, so its re-entry is `995 × 0.005 / 5.00 = 0.995000`. **D2 and the corrected run therefore land on the same bar and the same price and differ only in quantity — `0.994000` against `0.995000`** — which is the exact economic residue of filling a protective stop at the bar close instead of at the standing stop, carried forward into the next position's size.
  7. **Expected end state / reasons:** typed reason `POST_EXIT_COOLDOWN`; the absence of a decision on bar 4 must produce **no** entry in any arm, proving that cooldown expiry alone does not entitle an entry. Legacy arms are labelled `legacy, non-canonical`. End states: D0 long `0.994000` from bar 2; C2 long `0.994000` from bar 3; D2 long `0.994000` from bar 5; corrected long `0.995000` from bar 5.
  8. **Discriminator (RED):** three REDs. **(i)** C2 against D2 — same delay value, different first admissible bar, from the operator alone (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/runner.py:1305-1308`). **(ii)** C2 against the corrected run — an entry at bar 3 on a bar whose own raw signal did not decide it (`:1393-1409`). **(iii)** D2 against the corrected run — the quantity `0.994000` where the decided rule requires `0.995000`, a divergence that no event-level comparison would catch and that only the asserted quantity exposes.

### C34 — `tw_margin_call_mode`

- **A behaviour:** a required string validated to `{"off", "tradingview"}` (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/config.py:61`, `:285`, `:484-485`). The enabled branch runs only when `tw_audit_semantics_mode == "research"` (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/runner.py:563-564`); it probes the bar open and then the adverse extreme, closing a computed fraction at each checkpoint (`:1411-1428`, `:1430-1443`, `:1445-1450`). The margin percentages it uses are **derived from `max_leverage_cap`**, not supplied by a venue (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/config.py:619-624`).
- **B behaviour:** an independent approximation with the hard-coded maintenance multipliers `1.104` (long) and `0.97` (short) (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/engine/mtc_runner.py:36`, `:644-649`, `:694-705`), probing an inferred intrabar path (`:2393-2413`).
- **Pine reference:** a constant `"off"` with no consumer, and no margin declaration in `strategy()` (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/01_PINE/MTC_V2.pine:7`, `:98`).
- **Disagreement:** two different fabricated liquidation models, neither anchored to a venue.
- **Canonical semantics and reason:** reproduce `off` and A's enabled legacy branch, then retire the key in favour of C10's frozen venue margin model. A liquidation with no venue schedule is `MARGIN_MODEL_UNAVAILABLE`.
- **Chosen implementation:** **RETIRE**.
- **GF-34**
  1. **Type:** bar-driven and **fully asserted for every run, L2 included.** The round-3 revision gave L2 the record-it-later type (§1.4) and deferred its tuple. That deferral is withdrawn, and withdrawing it exposed two defects in the round-3 arm that a recording would have silently absorbed — a configuration A cannot express, and a bar sequence that could not reach the branch. Both are repaired here.

     **What was wrong.** Round-3 field 2 set `sizing_method: FIXED_QTY, requested_fixed_qty: 20`. **A has no `FIXED_QTY` sizer:** `calc_qty` offers risk-at-stop and a percent-of-equity fallback and nothing else (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/position_sizer.py:43-55`), and A's `fixed_qty` key is rejected unless it equals `1.0` (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/config.py:457-462`). Worse, **even granting the intended `20 @ 100.00`, the round-3 bars could not fire the branch.** With `initial_capital 1000` and a derived margin fraction of `0.20`, the deficit at a mark of `m` is `required − equity = 0.20 × 20 × m − (1000 + 20 × (m − 100)) = 1000 − 16m` (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/runner.py:1449-1459`), which is positive only for `m < 62.50`. The lowest price anywhere in the round-3 sequence was `84`. L2 would have recorded "no margin call", the fixture would have shown legacy and canonical agreeing, and the row's entire claim — that the legacy branch fabricates a loss — would have gone untested.
  2. **Config:** L1 `{tw_audit_semantics_mode: "off", tw_margin_call_mode: "off"}`; L2 `{tw_audit_semantics_mode: "research", tw_margin_call_mode: "tradingview"}`; control run L3 `{tw_audit_semantics_mode: "off", tw_margin_call_mode: "tradingview"}`. V1 corrected-vNext with **no** `venue_margin_schedule`; V2 corrected-vNext with the schedule in field 3. All runs `initial_capital: 1000`, `max_leverage_cap: 5.0`, `use_sl: false`, `use_sl_percent: false`, `use_sl_atr: false`, `use_sl_swing_atr: false`, `tp_mode: "None"`, **`fallback_size_pct: 400.0`**, `cooldown_bars: 0`, `max_entries: 1`, all gates disabled. The entry quantity is produced by A's own fallback path rather than by a method A does not have: with no stop, `raw_qty = equity × (fallback_size_pct / 100) / entry = 1000 × 4.0 / 100 = 40`, under a leverage cap of `(1000 × 5.0) / (100 × 1) = 50` that does not bind, giving **exactly `40`** (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/position_sizer.py:48-55`). The entry is admissible: required margin `100 × 40 × 0.20 = 800` against a threshold of `1000 × 5.0 = 5000` (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/runner.py:1490-1502`).
  3. **Frozen metadata:** `instrument_price_tick 0.01`, `instrument_qty_step 1.0`, `instrument_min_qty 0`, `instrument_min_notional 0`, `instrument_contract_multiplier 1`. Derived A margin percentage at `max_leverage_cap 5.0` is `100/5 = 20.0`% for both sides, i.e. `margin_frac = 0.20` (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/config.py:619-624`). V2's `venue_margin_schedule` is `{initial_margin_rate: 0.20, maintenance_margin_rate: 0.10, mark_price_source: "bar_close", liquidation_fee_rate: 0.005, liquidation_rule: "PARTIAL_TO_MAINTENANCE"}` — the maintenance rate, mark source, fee rate and rule are **GF-10.B's, unchanged**; only the initial-margin rate differs, at `0.20` rather than GF-10.B's `0.50`, because GF-10.B deliberately runs an under-collateralised bucket and this row needs the `40 @ 100.00` entry to be admissible (`40 × 100 × 0.20 = 800 <= 1000`).
  4. **Bars:** `0 | 2026-01-01T00:00:00Z | 100,100,100,100,10` raw `(1,0)`; `1 | 2026-01-01T00:01:00Z | 98,98,94,95,10` raw `(0,0)`; `2 | 2026-01-01T00:02:00Z | 94,94,88,89,10` raw `(0,0)`; `3 | 2026-01-01T00:03:00Z | 88,88,84,85,10` raw `(0,0)`. The sequence is chosen against the legacy branch's own threshold. At quantity `40` the required margin at a mark of `m` is `0.20 × 40 × m = 8m` and the equity at that mark is `1000 + 40 × (m − 100) = 40m − 3000`, so the deficit is `8m − (40m − 3000) = 3000 − 32m`, which is positive exactly when **`m < 93.75`**. The four checkpoints the branch actually probes, in order, are `98`, `94`, `94`, `88`; the first three sit **above** `93.75` — bar 1's low of `94` deliberately by a quarter of a point — and bar 2's low of `88` is the first below it. The sequence therefore exercises three non-firing checkpoints and one firing checkpoint rather than asserting a single number.
  5. **Expected events, all asserted.**
     - **L1** and **L3** — no margin exit on any bar; the long position opened at bar 0 is still open at bar 3. L3 is asserted **identical to L1 field for field**, proving the master gate (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/runner.py:563-564`).
     - **L2** — the branch probes each bar's open and then, for a long, its low when the low is below the open (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/runner.py:1430-1443`), and the fixture asserts the deficit at **every** checkpoint, not only the firing one. Bar 1, mark `98`: required `0.20 × 40 × 98 = 784.00`, equity `1000 + 40 × (98 − 100) = 920.00`, deficit **`−136.00`** → no exit. Bar 1, mark `94`: required `752.00`, equity `760.00`, deficit **`−8.00`** → no exit. Bar 2, mark `94` (the bar open): deficit **`−8.00`** again → no exit. Bar 2, mark `88` (the bar low): required `0.20 × 40 × 88 = 704.00`, equity `1000 + 40 × (88 − 100) = 520.00`, deficit **`+184.00`** → **`MARGIN_CALL` fires at bar 2 against the low `88.00`**. Bar 3 is not reached with a position.
     - **V1** — `MARGIN_MODEL_UNAVAILABLE` recorded once at bar 0 and **no** liquidation on any bar.
     - **V2** — the decided venue rule marks to each bar's close and compares equity against maintenance: bar 1 `800.00 >= 380.00`; bar 2 `560.00 >= 356.00`; bar 3 `400.00 >= 340.00`. **No breach on any bar, so no liquidation event and no partial.** V2 is asserted identical to V1 apart from the absence of the `MARGIN_MODEL_UNAVAILABLE` record, and is unchanged whether `tw_margin_call_mode` is present or absent.
  6. **Expected fills/prices/quantities:** every run's bar-0 entry is `40 @ 100.00`. L1, L3, V1 and V2 have **exactly that one fill** and end with the position open at `40`. L2 has a second fill, asserted in full: at bar 2, mark `88.00`, the branch computes `liquidation_qty = (184.00 × 4.0) / (88.00 × 1 × 0.20) = 736 / 17.6 = 41.818181…`, clamps it to the position quantity `40` (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/runner.py:1463-1464`), and divides by the reference quantity `40` — the position quantity, because no working-exit book exists under `tp_mode: "None"` and `use_sl: false` (`:1465-1472`) — giving `exit_pct = 1.0`. The "partial" liquidation is therefore a **full close of `40 @ 88.00`**, realising `40 × (88.00 − 100.00) = −480.00` and leaving equity `520.00`. The `4.0` multiplier that produces the over-liquidation is a hard-coded guess whose own comment says so (`:1462`).
  7. **Expected end state / reasons:** typed reasons `margin_call` (A's literal legacy label, `MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/runner.py:105`), `MARGIN_MODEL_UNAVAILABLE`, `LIQUIDATION_PARTIAL`. End states: L1, L3, V1, V2 — long `40` open, realised `0.00`, equity at the bar-3 close `400.00`. L2 — **flat**, realised `−480.00`, equity `520.00`, labelled `legacy, non-canonical`.
  8. **Discriminator (RED):** **(i)** L2 against V2 — the identical four bars, the identical position, and the identical account produce a **full liquidation at `88.00` realising `−480.00`** under the legacy branch and **no liquidation at all** under the decided venue rule, whose worst coverage over the whole sequence is `400.00` against a requirement of `340.00`. This is the row's claim made falsifiable: the legacy loss is not a modelled venue event, it is manufactured by a leverage-derived margin fraction and a `4.0` multiplier with no venue behind either (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/config.py:619-624`; `MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/runner.py:1462-1463`). **(ii)** L3 against L2 — identical `tw_margin_call_mode`, opposite outcome, proving the master gate. **(iii)** V1 against B, whose hard-coded `1.104` / `0.97` maintenance multipliers liquidate with no schedule present at all (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/engine/mtc_runner.py:644-649`).

### C35 — `tw_margin_call_split_entries`

**Citation corrected in repair round 1.** The previous revision supported the "stamped but no consumer" claim with a non-existent architecture-audit file. The claim is now cited to the two lines that actually read the key.

- **Disagreement:** the key's name implies a margin-call splitting branch that does not exist in any of the three implementations. Fabricating one is prohibited.
- **Canonical semantics and reason:** retire it as a non-capability, after **proving** both boolean values are behaviourally identical. It does not survive into canonical configuration. If any economic difference is found, the disposition fails and the investigation reopens.
- **Chosen implementation:** **RETIRE**, with no fabricated branch.

On bar 3 the stop is `103.00`; the open `106` is not beyond it and the low `102` crosses it. Exit **bar 3 @ `103.00`**.

### C38 — Swing-break/pivot confirmation state machine (the third behaviour)

**New row, added in repair round 1.** The previous revision described confirmation as a two-way A-versus-B disagreement inside C03/C04. That hid a third, economically active behaviour: B implements a full pivot swing-break plus momentum state machine that has no counterpart in A or Pine, while A ships a same-named module that is a **deliberate no-op scaffold**. Presenting the scaffold as an implementation would have made the table read as though A had this capability.

- **A behaviour — an inert scaffold, not an implementation.** `mtc_v2/core/confirmation.py` carries a state container, a snapshot type and three step functions, and its own docstring says the module "is intentionally non-invasive by default" and that the real FSM "can be filled in later" (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/confirmation.py:8-28`). Every step function returns the raw signal **unchanged**: disabled returns it immediately, and enabled records bookkeeping fields and still returns it unchanged (`:93-114`, `:117-131`, `:134-152`). The runner wires it into the per-bar path (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/runner.py:282-285`, `:353-361`), and configuration declares and validates **thirteen** `l18b_*` keys plus `use_l18b_confirmation` — **fourteen keys in total** (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/config.py:184-197`, validated one-for-one at `:403-416`). **The count was `fifteen` in every prior revision and that was wrong by one:** `use_l18b_confirmation` sits at `:184` and the thirteen `l18b_*` keys occupy `:185-197` (`grep -c '^    "l18b_' MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/00_PYTHON/mtc_v2/core/config.py` returns `13`), so line 184 was being counted once as the master flag and once as an `l18b_*` key. The thirteen, in declaration order, are `l18b_swing_left`, `l18b_swing_right`, `l18b_timeout_bars`, `l18b_min_wait_bars`, `l18b_dynamic_level_while_waiting`, `l18b_require_close_beyond`, `l18b_close_buffer_pct`, `l18b_raw_event_mode`, `l18b_tie_rule`, `l18b_use_momentum`, `l18b_momentum_mode`, `l18b_confirm_session_filter`, `l18b_refresh_on_new_raw`. **Setting `use_l18b_confirmation: true` and every one of the thirteen changes no economic result**, because no branch consumes the recorded state.
- **B behaviour — the only real implementation, restated from source in repair round 6 on the two points the previous revision got wrong.** `ConfirmationLayer` precomputes Pine-style confirmed pivots with a `left`/`right` window and publishes each pivot on its **confirmation** bar — the pivot at index `i − right` becomes visible at bar `i`, never earlier, and the loop starts only at `i = left + right` (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/modules/confirmation_layer.py:13-34`, `:104-105`). Its `step` is a genuine FSM (`:175-410`) with session gating and a flat-only option (`:200-201`), a maximum pivot age (`:213-226`), a maximum swing distance test (`:37-54`, `:281`, `:295`), dynamic level updates while waiting under `ANY` or `TIGHTEN_ONLY` (`:312-336`), a confirmation timeout (`:338-348`), a tick break buffer (`:350-351`), a minimum wait age (`:352-353`), and an eval-start alignment path (`:145-173`). The runner calls it before entry-mode shaping and consumes `long_confirmed` / `short_confirmed` plus the waiting flags (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/engine/mtc_runner.py:1206-1218`). Its full surface is declared at `MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/config/defaults.py:36-68`.
- **B behaviour — the wait-creation precondition, which the round-5 fixture violated.** A wait opens only when a **raw event coexists with an already-published pivot**: `take_long_event and pivot_age_ok_high` (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/modules/confirmation_layer.py:276`), where `pivot_age_ok_high` requires `last_swing_high is not None` (`:213-219`) and `last_swing_high` is set only from a non-`NaN` published pivot (`:206-208`). A raw event that arrives **before** the pivot's confirmation bar therefore opens nothing at all, silently, and — in the default `EDGE` mode — no later bar re-offers it, because a level that stays true produces no second edge (`:228`, `:233-238`). The short side is the mirror (`:290`, `:220-226`, `:209-211`).
- **B behaviour — the same-bar tie rule, restated correctly.** `same_bar_tie_rule` resolves **two simultaneous raw events**, and it does so **before any wait is created**: when `take_long_event and take_short_event` are both true, `LONG_WINS` clears the short event, `SHORT_WINS` clears the long event, and `IGNORE` clears **both** (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/modules/confirmation_layer.py:265-274`), after which the surviving event or events reach the wait-creation blocks at `:276` and `:290`. **It has nothing to do with simultaneous breaks;** the break tests run later and independently on each side (`:355-361`), and a wait on one side always nulls the other side's level when it opens (`:283-285`, `:297-299`), so two simultaneous breaks cannot arise from one tie in the first place. The round-5 description — "`LONG_WINS` as resolving simultaneous *breaks*" — is withdrawn.
- **B behaviour — one declared key is provably inert, and repair round 6 records it rather than repeating the round-5 claim about it.** `defer_break_on_level_update` cannot defer anything as implemented: `allow_break_long = (not defer) or (not long_level_updated) or long_break_raw` and `break_long = … and allow_break_long and long_break_raw` (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/modules/confirmation_layer.py:357`, `:360`), so whenever `long_break_raw` is true the third disjunct makes `allow_break_long` true, and whenever it is false `break_long` is already false — `allow_break_long` never changes an outcome. A second, independent reason reinforces it on the long side: a pivot published at bar `i` is by construction the maximum of a window that **includes** bar `i` (`:20-30`), so the newly published level is always at or above that bar's close and `close > level` cannot hold on the bar the level was published. The key is therefore dispositioned exactly as C35's `tw_margin_call_split_entries` is — retired as a non-capability, with an **inverted RED** that reopens the disposition if any run ever differs on it.
- **Pine reference:** Pine's L18 block is the same **N-bar count transform** that A implements (C03), not a pivot FSM (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/01_MTC_PROJECT/01_PINE/MTC_V2.pine:1684-1739`). Pine's own `l18b_*` fragment operates on the count-transform state, not on pivots (`:1709-1718`).
- **Disagreement:** this is not a difference of parameters. **Three behaviours exist:** the A/Pine N-bar count transform (C03), B's pivot swing-break FSM (this row), and A's inert `l18b_*` scaffold that resembles the second but does nothing. Both of corpus B's unresolved cases are in this capability — case `402 parity_bnd_211_swing_right_bars_v03` maps to `confirmation.p_right = 14` under the parent input "Use Confirmation: Swing Break + Momentum = On" (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/parity_suite_350/cases/parity_bnd_211_swing_right_bars_v03.json:115-145`), and case `416 parity_bnd_217_dynamic_update_mode_v02` maps to `confirmation.dyn_update_mode = "ANY"` with `dynamic_level_while_waiting = true` (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/parity_suite_350/cases/parity_bnd_217_dynamic_update_mode_v02.json:115-135`).
- **Canonical semantics and reason:** the pivot confirmation state machine is a **real economic capability and is retained**, with B as the semantic source, corrected on three points: (i) the same-bar tie rule must be explicit and may not default to a direction — `IGNORE` is the canonical default, because B's shipped `LONG_WINS` (`MTC_COMMAND_CENTER/02_MTC_BACKTEST/src/config/defaults.py:61`) silently biases a symmetric strategy; (ii) a raw event that arrives while **no pivot has been published** must emit a typed `PIVOT_UNCONFIRMED` block rather than being silently dropped, and an aged-out pivot must emit `PIVOT_AGED_OUT` rather than passing through; (iii) `defer_break_on_level_update` is **retired as a non-capability** — the decided rule states the deferral it names in one place or not at all, and does not ship a key that cannot change a result. **A's `l18b_*` scaffold is retired in full**: **fourteen** configuration keys that cannot change any result are configuration debt, and a key that looks economic but is not is worse than an absent key. Retiring them is a disposition, not a fabrication — no behaviour is removed, because none exists.
- **Chosen implementation:** **B, corrected** for the tie rule, the fail-closed pivot and the inert deferral key; **RETIRE** `use_l18b_confirmation` and the **thirteen** `l18b_*` keys — fourteen names in total — from the kernel configuration.

Bar 8's own close of `103` breaks neither. Inverted RED: any difference falsifies the retirement and reopens the investigation.

The `SHORT_WINS` arm is the mirror and lands on a **different bar and a different price** (`1 @ 91.00` at bar 8), so the three arms are separated on two axes rather than one.

This is the arm the round-5 fixture asserted in prose and could not have produced, because its table published no second pivot while a wait was open.
